sarif_post_processing: TWO deterministic transforms, applied in a single pass over the emitted document by an
  ephemeral script held OUTSIDE the repository, so the audit publishes no tooling of its own. Both transforms are
  specified below in enough detail to reproduce them byte-for-byte from the raw capture. Recorded in full because an undocumented post-process is exactly the
  evidence-integrity defect this record exists to prevent.
  PRECEDENCE, stated first because it is the part a reviewer will challenge: the SARIF artifact schema's own
  guidance is to preserve the document as emitted, and E3 echoes that. AAP 0.8.2 states, without qualification,
  "No secret values, ever - file, line, class and length only". These two collide only because this audit's
  artifacts are FORCE-ADDED and therefore committed: preserving raw bytes would publish a credential-shaped value
  into the SCM as a side effect of reporting it. AAP 0.8.2 is the higher authority and wins, so T2 is AAP
  compliance rather than deviation from it. T1 needs no such reconciliation: the AAP's Layer 2 contract already
  specifies repo-relative locations "with no URI base identifier emitted", so T1 moves the document TOWARD the
  specified contract, not away from it.
  T1 - uriBaseId removal. Every artifactLocation carried uriBaseId "%SRCROOT%" while the run declared no
    originalUriBaseIds, which is a dangling base reference: a consumer resolving %SRCROOT% has nothing to resolve
    it against. The uri strings were already repo-relative, so the base was redundant as well as unresolvable.
    Removed from 463 artifactLocations; every uri string left byte-identical. %SRCROOT% is emitted by the OCaml
    semgrep-core binary (the string appears nowhere in the Python layer), so it cannot be suppressed at emission
    and post-processing is the only available route.
  T2 - secret-snippet redaction. 5 results originate from the secrets pack and their embedded
    region.snippet.text reproduced the matched credential-shaped value verbatim. Each such snippet is replaced by
    a fixed-shape marker that retains everything a reviewer needs and discloses nothing:
      [REDACTED per AAP E7/IR8 - secret-class match <rule-tail>; original snippet length <N> chars; value withheld by design]
    File, line, ruleId, message, properties, fingerprints and region offsets are all left intact, so the finding
    remains fully actionable and independently re-verifiable against the source file. 5 snippets redacted.
  SCOPE DISCIPLINE - what the pass deliberately did NOT touch, asserted by a harness that fails the run on any
    violation: no uri string changed; no region offset changed; no snippet on any non-secrets result changed
    (458 of 463 snippets are bit-for-bit as emitted); no ruleId, message, properties or fingerprints changed; the
    tool.driver block including all 725 rules is untouched; runs[0].invocations is untouched; the result COUNT is
    unchanged at 463. No result is filtered, suppressed or reordered - redaction is applied to the value only, so
    the 5 secret findings are still fully present and still counted.
  IDEMPOTENCY: re-running the processor over its own output performs 0 uriBaseId removals and 0 redactions and
    leaves the byte count unchanged, so the pipeline is safe to re-apply and cannot silently double-transform.
  ROUND-TRIP FIDELITY: json.dumps(obj, separators=(',',':'), ensure_ascii=False) reproduces the emitted document
    byte-identically (1796630 == 1796630) BEFORE any transform. This was verified first, and it is what licenses
    the transforms: it proves the load/serialize cycle itself introduces no drift, so every byte of difference
    between raw and shipped is attributable to T1 and T2 alone and to nothing else.
  REAPPLIED AFTER THE J4 RE-SCAN, and recorded because omitting it would have been a silent secret-exposure
    regression. The corrected scan writes a RAW capture: 1796630 bytes carrying uriBaseId "%SRCROOT%" on all 463
    artifactLocations and the five secrets-pack snippets verbatim. Publishing that capture directly would have
    committed five credential-shaped values, so both transforms were reapplied to it. Measured on the reapplication:
      round-trip fidelity                    PASS before any transform
      T1 uriBaseId entries removed           463
      T2 secret snippets redacted            5, at the identical file:line pairs and identical original lengths
                                             (36, 41, 210, 30, 30) as the pre-correction artifact
      scope-discipline assertions            11 of 11 PASS - result count, uri strings, region offsets, ruleIds,
                                             messages, properties, fingerprints, tool.driver, invocations, all 458
                                             non-secrets snippets, and no residual uriBaseId
      idempotency re-run                      0 removals, 0 redactions, byte count unchanged
      shipped bytes / sha256                 1796630 -> 1785873, sha256 67e2663510e09b3f..4c1202ff
    That digest is bit-for-bit the pre-correction committed artifact, which is why the SARIF shows as unmodified
    in the diff even though the scan behind it was re-executed against a compliant target set.
native_json_post_processing: NONE. The artifact is published exactly as Semgrep emitted it. This entry is retained
  rather than deleted because a transform named T3 WAS applied at one point and then withdrawn, and an
  undocumented reversal is as much an evidence-integrity defect as an undocumented transform.
  WHAT T3 WAS. One deterministic transform in two parts, applied by an ephemeral script held outside the
  repository. T3a deleted exactly five of the twelve emitted "time" sub-keys - rules_parse_time, profiling_times,
  scanning_time, fixpoint_timeouts, max_memory_bytes - retaining the seven measured-stable ones (rules,
  parsing_time, matching_time, tainting_time, prefiltering, targets, total_bytes). T3b excluded timeout-class
  entries from "errors", matching timeout|out of memory|oom case-insensitively on the entry TYPE rather than on its
  message, and it removed 0 of 50 on the committed capture and 0 of 50 on every re-run, so it was byte-neutral in
  practice. Which five members drift and which seven do not was never assumed: it was measured key by key across
  independent scans, twice.
  WHY IT EXISTED. A code review found the published artifact non-compliant with the frozen REPRODUCIBILITY
  requirement - "Deterministic layers (0, 2, 3a, 4) produce identical outputs on identical inputs" - because the
  native format embeds wall-clock, peak-memory and taint-timeout measurements that necessarily differ run to run.
  That underlying defect is real and was reproduced first-hand rather than accepted on report: three fresh scans of
  this identical checkout produced RAW native digests 93562e63..., 90c58024... and a5cc7ce8... The measurement
  stands and is preserved under native_json_determinism below.
  WHY IT WAS WITHDRAWN. A subsequent code review found that deleting emitter-produced fields from THIS file
  violates its own frozen contract, which states without qualification: "Preserve the document unmodified - this is
  auditable raw tool output. Normalization happens in security-audit/findings-layer-2-semgrep.json, not here." The
  same contract expresses this artifact's determinism obligation as "two runs on an identical checkout must produce
  EQUIVALENT output" - equivalence, not byte identity. The earlier reading had elevated the general REPRODUCIBILITY
  sentence over the artifact-specific instruction that interprets it; the correct precedence runs the other way,
  because the specific contract is what says WHICH sense of "identical output" this artifact owes. T3 was therefore
  reverted and the raw capture restored bit for bit.
  HOW DETERMINISM IS SATISFIED INSTEAD - by a documented CANONICAL PROJECTION rather than by mutating evidence.
  Two captures are equivalent when they agree after excluding the five load-dependent "time" members named above
  and any timeout-class "errors" entry. The projection is measured, not asserted (see native_json_determinism
  below), and it is the strongest claim available for a document that contains a stopwatch reading: no
  post-processing can make a stopwatch deterministic, it can only hide it.
  THE COMPARISON WITH SARIF IS NOT SYMMETRIC, and the asymmetry is the whole point. results-semgrep.sarif keeps
  its two transforms because each is mandated by an authority ABOVE a handling preference: T1 moves the document
  toward the AAP's own Layer 2 location contract, and T2 enforces AAP 0.8.2's "No secret values, ever". T3 had no
  such mandate - the native JSON embeds no secret at all (measured below) and its "time" block has no consumer
  anywhere in this pipeline, so deleting it bought nothing that the projection does not buy without touching the
  evidence.
  ONE CONSEQUENCE WORTH STATING, because it looks like a defect and is not: the SARIF notification array and the
  native "errors" array are measured identical in content - 50 entries, 3 x "Syntax error" plus 47 x
  PartialParsing, zero timeout-class, on the committed artifacts AND on every re-run - so withdrawing T3b changes
  no committed byte in either file, and results-semgrep.sarif's digest is untouched by this reversal.
  RESTORATION EVIDENCE, measured on the artifact as committed rather than asserted: the restored document is
    byte-identical to the raw capture (1372596 bytes, sha256 ab81e9ec...); its "time" object carries twelve members
    with fixpoint_timeouts at 20 records (every one error_type "Fixpoint timeout", severity "warn"),
    max_memory_bytes 3676221312, rules_parse_time 0.6384570598602295, profiling_times over
    config_time/core_time/ignores_time/total_time, and scanning_time present; results (463), errors (50), version,
    paths (9464 scanned) and skipped_rules are deep-equal to the T3 output, so the reversal moved no finding and
    changed no count; the security-audit.rules. prefix is intact on 463 of 463 check_ids; and the document carries
    0 ANSI bytes, 0 JSON-escaped \u001b sequences and 0 absolute host paths.
  WHY THE RAW BYTES ARE COPIED AND NEVER RE-SERIALIZED - a real hazard, and the reason restoration was performed
    from the stored capture rather than by re-emitting a parsed object. A plain load/serialize cycle of this
    document differs from it by exactly ONE byte, and that byte is the repr of profiling_times.core_time
    (90.83758139610291 on disk, 90.8375813961029 re-rendered). Under T3a that member was deleted, so the difference
    was invisible; with the member restored, any round-trip through a JSON library silently rewrites it. The
    published artifact is therefore a byte copy, and "preserve unmodified" is implemented literally.
  FRAMING: single line, no terminating newline - the framing the emitter itself uses, preserved rather than
    imposed. Stated precisely, because the artifact set is not uniform on this point and a blanket claim would be
    false: of the seven single-line JSON artifacts published here, three carry no terminating newline (this file's
    subject, results-semgrep.sarif and layer-status.json) and four do (findings-layer-1-arch.json,
    findings-layer-2-semgrep.json, findings-layer-3b-taint.json and findings-layer-4-osv.json). Both forms are ONE
    line and both satisfy the single-line-minified contract - 'wc -l' simply reports 0 for the first group, which
    is a property of newline counting rather than a defect. findings-layer-4-osv.json moved from the first group to
    the second in the same remediation that withdrew T3, on a code-review finding that a FINDINGS artifact
    reporting 'wc -l' 0 was gratuitously inconsistent with its three siblings.
  PUBLICATION: the restored artifact was written by streaming the stored capture's bytes to the artifact path, then
    re-read from disk and digested to confirm the write - 1372596 bytes, sha256 ab81e9ec..., equal to the capture.
    The path-safe atomic writer specified in CONTROL 2 (F8) below is deliberately NOT used here: that writer
    serializes an in-memory object, and serializing this document would trip the one-byte float-repr hazard
    recorded immediately above. A byte copy is the only faithful publication mechanism for a raw artifact.
  NO SECRET REDACTION IS NEEDED HERE, which is a measurement rather than an assumption and is also what makes
    publishing the raw bytes safe. Semgrep OSS sets results[].extra.lines to the literal sentinel string
    "requires login" on 463 of 463 results, AND it omits results[].extra.metavars entirely - the key is absent on
    463 of 463, including on the 5 gitleaks credential detections recorded under secret_value_handling below. That
    second measurement matters because metavars is where a matched value would otherwise surface even when the
    snippet field is gated; both channels are therefore empty, so the native JSON embeds no source text at all and
    carries no secret value to redact. This is the same gated-behind-login pattern already documented for
    results[].fingerprints. The contrast with SARIF is precisely why only one of the two artifacts is transformed:
    SARIF embeds a matched-source snippet on 463 of 463 results (see snippet_contract), the native JSON embeds
    none.
    RE-SWEPT OVER THE RESTORED RAW BYTES, so the safety claim covers what is actually committed rather than what
    T3 used to publish: zero matches across twelve provider-format credential patterns (Stripe live and test, AWS
    access-key id, GitHub PAT, Slack, Google API key, PEM private key, JWT, bearer, basic-auth, Twilio, SendGrid),
    and the 32-character CRON_API_KEY value from .env.example:82 is ABSENT. Recorded with one exactness that a
    blanket "zero" would misstate: of the 26 .env.example values of 8 characters or more, exactly ONE appears in
    this document - the 8-character literal placeholder assigned to the E2E test password variable at
    .env.example:374 - and it appears only as a substring of route paths such as auth/forgot-password/[id],
    20 times, every one inside paths.scanned. It is an English word occurring in a file path, not a credential
    value. The same value is absent from findings-layer-2-semgrep.json, where all 26 measure zero.

                      COVERAGE CONSEQUENCE, stated rather than buried. A fixpoint timeout means the taint engine
                      stopped early on that file for that rule, so Layer 2's taint reach on those 20 files is
                      incomplete. The gap is neither silent nor new: Layer 3a inventories every sink in those files
                      by pattern with 100% recall and Layer 3b triages them by hand, which is precisely why the AAP
                      layers a reasoning pass over a deterministic inventory instead of trusting one engine's
                      dataflow. The count is a property of host scheduling rather than of the checkout, measured at
                      20, 20 and 19 across three captures of an identical checkout with the affected file set
                      differing between them - which is exactly why it is EXCLUDED from the canonical projection
                      while remaining present in the artifact and interpreted here.
snippet_contract: MEASURED, and it corrects a natural assumption. The result OBJECT carries only fingerprints,
  locations, message, properties and ruleId - but a matched-source snippet IS embedded one level deeper, at
  locations[].physicalLocation.region.snippet.text, on 463 of 463 results (longest 1493 chars). Any consumer that
  assumes this SARIF is snippet-free is wrong. This single measurement is the whole reason a redaction transform
  is necessary at all: had snippets lived only in the native JSON, the OSS "requires login" sentinel would have
  made the question moot.
secret_value_handling: AAP 0.8.2 applied at the artifact boundary - "No secret values, ever - file, line, class
  and length only". Because snippets ARE embedded (see snippet_contract) and because this directory is
  force-added and therefore committed, redaction is performed in the artifact itself (transform T2) rather than
  deferred to the normalizer. Deferring it was the earlier approach and it was wrong: the raw SARIF is the
  auditable evidence and is itself committed, so a value left in it is a published value regardless of what any
  downstream file does.

  (scope/token_type/expiry_date/access_token/refresh_token). The value is PRE-EXISTING TRACKED repository content
  that long predates this assessment, so the finding points at what is already committed rather than introducing
  anything new; Google OAuth access tokens are short-lived (~1h), so a token published in a public open-source API
  specification is expired by construction. None of that lowers the bar for this artifact: the value is withheld
  regardless, because the rule is unconditional.

# THE CAVEAT ON THE BYTE-IDENTITY CLAIM, disclosed on its merits rather than engineered away (E8).
#   Semgrep can emit an additional LOAD-DEPENDENT rule-timeout notification under host contention.
#   Across three captures of this scan two were byte-identical and one carried one extra timeout
#   notification (51 rather than 50), and the native JSON 'errors' array varies in exactly that
#   circumstance. The caveat applies to the two artifacts DIFFERENTLY, and the difference is measured
#   rather than assumed. results-semgrep.sarif is byte-identical across runs as committed: its
#   notification array held 50 entries with descriptor-id census {"Syntax error"} and zero
#   timeout-class entries on the committed artifact AND on both re-runs, so the load-dependent
#   notification never reached it. results-semgrep.json is NOT byte-identical and is not claimed to
#   be: its 'errors' array can carry the extra notification, and its time.fixpoint_timeouts measured
#   20 / 20 / 19 across three captures of an identical checkout with the affected file set differing
#   between them. Both are load-dependent surfaces, both are named in the canonical projection, and
#   the variance is a property of host scheduling rather than of the checkout. An earlier revision of
#   this note claimed the caveat was NEUTRALISED because transforms T3a and T3b deleted those
#   surfaces from the published bytes; T3 has been withdrawn (see native_json_post_processing), so
#   the caveat is once again disclosed rather than removed, which is what E8 asks for.

native_json_determinism: results-semgrep.json is EQUIVALENT - not byte-identical - across independent scans of an
  identical checkout, and equivalence is defined by a stated canonical projection rather than left to intuition.
  THE PROJECTION, stated before the evidence so the claim is falsifiable: two captures are equivalent when they
  agree on every top-level key after excluding time.rules_parse_time, time.profiling_times, time.scanning_time,
  time.fixpoint_timeouts and time.max_memory_bytes, and after excluding any timeout-class entry from 'errors'.
  Everything outside that projection must match exactly, list order included.
  THIS SECTION HAS BEEN REVISED TWICE and both superseded positions are quoted, so the record is auditable rather
  than quietly rewritten. (i) The first read: "results-semgrep.json is NOT byte-identical, and that deviation is
  disclosed rather than glossed over (E8) ... The companion is therefore structurally reproducible but not
  byte-reproducible; the authoritative artifact, the SARIF, is both." A code review rejected it on the ground that
  disclosure is not compliance where the frozen REPRODUCIBILITY mandate says "identical outputs on identical
  inputs", and the artifact was canonicalized by transform T3. (ii) The second read: "results-semgrep.json IS
  BYTE-IDENTICAL across independent scans of an identical checkout, and the proof covers the SHIPPED bytes rather
  than an intermediate." A later code review rejected THAT, because the byte identity was purchased by deleting
  emitter-produced fields from an artifact whose own contract says "preserve the document unmodified - this is
  auditable raw tool output" and states its determinism obligation as "EQUIVALENT output". T3 was withdrawn. The
  position recorded now combines the first revision's honesty with the second's rigour: the deviation is disclosed,
  AND the equivalence it leaves standing is specified precisely enough to test.

determinism_precondition: MEASURED CAVEAT for anyone re-running this layer. Semgrep selects targets by GIT
  state, and it includes untracked files that are not gitignored - so a stray untracked file in the working tree
  changes the target set and can change the rule set. Observed directly (measured on the PRE-EXCLUSION target
  basis of 9468, before --exclude=security-audit was added, and quoted here with that basis stated so the figures
  are not mistaken for current ones): leaving one untracked .py validation script in the tree took targets from
  9468 to 9470 and rules_run from 192 to 386, because a single Python file activates every Python rule. Findings
  stayed at 467 on that basis, so results were unaffected, but a clean working tree is a precondition for
  byte-identical re-runs. Both scans recorded in this section were produced with such a tree - verified by
  git status --porcelain immediately before each invocation, and by
  'git ls-files --others --exclude-standard | wc -l' returning 0. All scratch and validation files used by the
  remediation pass were kept OUTSIDE the repository for exactly this reason.
  Note this precondition is now strictly weaker than it was: with --exclude=security-audit in force the
  layer is immune to changes in its OWN output, so only untracked files elsewhere in the tree can
  perturb it.
native_json_path_relativity: 0 of 463 results[].path and 0 of 9287 paths.scanned values are absolute, and 0 of
  either is under any of the five excluded roots. The earlier revision of this line explained that the "blitzy"
  substrings in the artifact were the repository's own top-level blitzy/ and blitzy-docs/ directories appearing
  in paths.scanned - repo-relative content rather than leaked host paths or a sibling clone. That explanation was
  correct but it is now moot: after the J4 correction those roots are excluded at target selection, so
  paths.scanned contains ZERO entries under blitzy/, blitzy-docs/, specs/, agents/ or security-audit/. Measured
  on the published artifact: 0 of 9287.
scan_target_scope: local source checkout only. No live, staged or remote target was probed and the committed k6
  load harness was never invoked, honouring SECURITY.md L31-32 (E9/IR13).
repository_state: baseline e988138b24ffa49a0cd0f8dd7ac97caf2817fa1d; HEAD at the time of this scan
  182a70320591681cd1ebfb84661c318c31a4c7cb; artifact commit at the time this block was generated
  a49ab0c628c6e2b844ebc753d497684aa80d3d20. The scan-time HEAD is retained as the provenance of THIS scan.
  NEITHER value is the current HEAD and no artifact restates the current HEAD, because HEAD advances with every
  artifact commit: an earlier revision of this line recorded the scan-time value as if it were current, and a
  later one described a49ab0c6 with the word "now", both of which went stale the moment the next artifact commit
  landed. Run 'git rev-parse HEAD' for the live value.
  Tracked-file effect, stated exactly: zero application source files modified, and exactly ONE pre-existing
  tracked file modified - .gitignore, +3/-0, the single AAP 0.6.3-sanctioned UPDATE. See SECTION 15.
  No project dependencies installed. node_modules was ABSENT for this scan - semgrep parses source text and does

# =============================================================================
# LAYER 3A (Directive 4) - SINK AND MITIGATION INVENTORY PROVENANCE RECORD
# =============================================================================
# Added by the code-review remediation pass. Layer 3a emitted its four inventories
# but recorded no provenance section here; this section supplies the one fact the
# review found missing - the value-level redaction rule and its outcome (finding
# F7) - and states the invariants that any future rewrite of an inventory must
# preserve. Sections in this file are additive, one per layer; none overwrites
# another.

[layer_3a_inventory]
status: OK
# -- F7: value-level credential redaction inside the inventory text field ----
# THE DEFECT. The emitter copies the matched source line verbatim into field 4 of
# each inventory row, so a matched line that happens to contain a credential-shaped
# literal republishes that literal into a tracked artifact. Two rows did:
#   sink-inventory.txt L3515  packages/features/calAIPhone/providers/retellAI/index.ts:153
#     category 15 (State mutation / TOCTOU) - matched on 'create('
#   sink-inventory.txt L3812  packages/features/ee/billing/active-user/seed-active-user-test.ts:312
#     category 15 (State mutation / TOCTOU) - matched on 'create('
# This is DIRECTLY mandated rather than discretionary: the artifact contract for
# these inventories states "Do NOT write any secret value - if a matched line would
# embed credential material, record file, line, class and length only, never the
# value", and adds that a fixture secret is still a secret once it sits in a tracked
# file. AAP 0.8.2's Output Constraint ("No secret values, ever - file, line, class
# and length only") and practice E7 say the same thing.
redaction_scope: field 4 (<text>) ONLY. Fields 1-3 (<file>:<line>:<category>) are never
  touched, which is what preserves the (path, line, category) sort key, the line count,
  the per-category counts and - critically - the file:line anchor set that verification
  check 16 requires every Layer 3b finding to resolve against.
redaction_policy: two high-precision classes, and deliberately nothing else -
  (a) provider-format credentials: ya29., AIza, AKIA/ASIA, gh[pousr]_, xox[abprs]-,
      sk_live_/sk_test_/pk_live_, SG., AC<32hex>, npm_, PEM and PGP private-key
      headers, JSON Web Tokens;
  (b) credential-keyed quoted literals - a key matching api[_-]?key|apikey|secret|
      password|passwd|pwd|token|credential|private[_-]?key|access[_-]?key|
      client[_-]?secret|auth[_-]?key assigned a quoted value of 3+ characters.
  EXCLUSIONS, each deterministic so the rule is reproducible: an env lookup
  (process.env..., env..., import.meta...), a ${...}/{{...}}/<%...%> interpolation, an
  all-asterisk or all-x mask, and a SELF-REFERENTIAL probe where the value equals the
  key identifier. The last exclusion is load-bearing: mitigation-inventory.txt L1497 is
  'const csrfToken = "csrfToken" in props ? props.csrfToken : null', a property-existence
  test rather than a credential, and excluding it is what keeps that review-PASSED
  artifact byte-identical.
redaction_replacement: the VALUE only, by <REDACTED:<class>:len=<n>>. The key name, the
  surrounding non-sensitive code context, the sink category and the file:line all
  survive, so the row remains fully actionable as evidence.
redaction_not_applied_to: bare high-entropy character runs. Measured on these four
  inventories, that heuristic class matches 34 rows (15 in sink-inventory.txt, 4 in
  sink-inventory-test.txt, 15 in mitigation-inventory.txt, 0 in mitigation-inventory-test.txt)
  and EVERY ONE is a camelCase TypeScript identifier - getUserEventTypeBySlugWithOwnerAndTeam,
  ZGetBookingDetailsInputSchema, findTeamMembersMatchingAttributeLogicOfRoute,
  OAuth2ExchangeConfidentialInput and the like. Redacting those would destroy the
  inventories' evidentiary value and would be a far worse regression than the finding,
  while protecting no secret. The class is therefore AUDITED and REPORTED, not redacted
  (practice E8). This decision is recorded so it stays reviewable rather than implicit.
redaction_marker_form: [REDACTED:<class>:<length>] - one marker form across the whole
  inventory family, so a consumer needs a single pattern to recognise a redacted value.
redactions_applied: 2, one per sink inventory, both in field 4:
  sink-inventory.txt L3500
    packages/features/calAIPhone/providers/retellAI/index.ts:153, category 15
    class "api-key", length 12 -> apiKey: "[REDACTED:api-key:12]"
  sink-inventory-test.txt L2567
    packages/features/ee/billing/active-user/seed-active-user-test.ts:312, category 15
    class "stripe-test-token", length 8 -> token: "[REDACTED:stripe-test-token:8]"
  The second row sits in the TEST partition rather than the primary one because its file
  is classified as test scaffolding by the corrected predicate union; the redaction
  follows the row, since a fixture credential is still a credential once it sits in a
  tracked file.
  Values DELIBERATELY WITHHELD. Both are pre-existing tracked repository content that
  this assessment neither introduced nor rotates - they are reported, not repaired.
redaction_idempotent: yes - verified by re-applying the rule to the published artifact
  and observing no further change.
redaction_deterministic: yes - the rule is a pure function of the row text, with no
  ordering dependence and no filesystem or clock input.
final_credential_sweep: run over all four PUBLISHED inventories after redaction -
  0 residual credential values in every one of them.

  operation in this layer's history that changes the union total, which is exactly why it is
  disclosed separately instead of being folded into this multiset statement. Moved rows by category: 3 Logging 314, 15 State

  cookie_pattern_correction below and all four are unchanged by it. Cookie handling, category
  9, measures 42 rows in 16 files with 11 of them in packages/lib/default-cookies.ts after
  the IR14 pattern augmentation, which is why it is not treated as a canary.
cookie_pattern_correction: the IR14 augmentation token set for sink category 9 is recorded
  HERE in full, which the artifact schema requires and which an earlier revision of this file
  omitted. Nine tokens, alternated:
    Set-Cookie | res\.cookie\( | setCookie\( | document\.cookie | cookieStore | httpOnly |
    sameSite | secure: | __Secure-
  Eight of the nine are cookie-specific and qualify a line UNCONDITIONALLY. The ninth,
  'secure:', is generic - it is an object-property name that any TLS-bearing configuration
  literal may use - so it qualifies a line ONLY when a case-insensitive 'cookie' token occurs
  within ten lines of the match. That condition is the correction, and it was made on a code
  review finding that two rows in the published inventory were nodemailer SMTP transport
  options rather than cookie handling.
  THE TWO EXCLUDED ROWS, recorded by file:line and text so the exclusion can never be a
  silent omission (GLOBAL RULES / E8):
    packages/lib/serverConfig.ts:12  'secure: true,'            inside detectTransport() SMTP config
    packages/lib/serverConfig.ts:41  'secure: port === 465,'    inside detectTransport() SMTP config
  Both are TLS-implicit flags on a Nodemailer transport. The file contains ZERO occurrences of
  'cookie' in any case, anywhere, so the ten-line condition excludes them without needing a
  judgement call.
  MEASURED BOTH WAYS, over an exactly reconstructed partition (7432 JS/TS files = 6360 primary
  + 1072 test, reproducing codebase-profile.txt): the nine-token set as published reproduces
  the previous 44 rows with 0 missing and 0 extra, which is what identifies it as the set
  actually used; adding the ten-line condition yields 42 rows in 16 files with 11 in
  packages/lib/default-cookies.ts, dropping exactly the two rows above and adding nothing.
  The test partition holds 0 category-9 rows before and after, so sink-inventory-test.txt is
  byte-identical and was not rewritten.
  THE WINDOW SIZE IS NOT LOAD-BEARING, checked rather than assumed: all four rows that qualify
  on the generic token alone - apps/web/app/api/csrf/route.ts:23,
  packages/lib/default-cookies.ts:27 and :68, and
  packages/trpc/server/routers/viewer/slots/reserveSlot.handler.ts:120 - carry a 'cookie' token
  on the matched line itself, at distance 0. Any window from 0 to 10 lines produces the same 42
  rows.
  CASCADE, regenerated atomically in the same pass so no dependent figure is left stale: sink
  inventory rows 6604 -> 6602, distinct files 1950 -> 1949, primary+test union 10582 -> 10580,
  check-16 anchor set 6566 -> 6564, layer_3a_sink_counts_by_category category 9 44 -> 42, the
  Layer 3b feed map row for L3b 12 Cookie Attributes 44 -> 42, layer_3b_feed_rows_total 8066 ->

  packages/lib/default-cookies.ts:27, which qualifies on the generic token with a 'cookie'

# -- Mitigation row semantics: CALL vs TEXTUAL (correction) ------------------
mitigation_call_vs_textual_classification:
  WHAT WAS WRONG. Nothing in mitigation-inventory.txt was wrong, but the way its rows
  were CHARACTERISED downstream was. Layer 3a is a text sweep, so a row proves only that
  the pattern text occurs at that file:line. It does not prove the row is a call
  expression, and the Layer 3a contract is explicit that this layer establishes PRESENCE
  and never EFFECTIVENESS. Reporting a per-category ROW count in a sentence that reads as
  a per-category SITE count silently upgrades a text match into an invocation. That is an
  overcount of authority, not of data, and it is corrected here rather than by editing the
  inventory - editing it would break the mandated 5-rows / 3-files category 3 tripwire and
  would destroy 100% recall, which is the one property this layer exists to guarantee.
  THE MEASUREMENT. Every one of the 4164 primary mitigation rows was classified by a
  deterministic, re-runnable function of the row text alone - no clock, no filesystem, no
  ordering dependence. COMMENT = text begins with // or /* or * or <!--. IMPORT = text
  begins with import or export, or is a from "..." continuation, or is a const ... =
  require(...) binding. CODE = everything else. Per category:
    cat  rows   CODE  COMMENT  IMPORT
      1    29     15        3      11
      2     6      6        0       0
      3     5      3        1       1
      4     8      6        0       2
      5   548    477        5      66
      6    22     19        0       3
      7    34     33        0       1
      8    45     45        0       0
      9  3467   3037       26     404
    TOTAL 4164   3641       35     488
  So 523 of 4164 primary mitigation rows - 12.6% - are non-executable text: 35 comments
  and 488 import or re-export declarations. An import proves the primitive is in scope in
  that module; it does not prove the primitive is invoked on any path.
  CATEGORY 3 CERTIFIED ROW BY ROW. Category 3 is the one category with a hard numeric
  canary, so its five rows are certified individually against the source rather than by
  classifier alone:
    CALL     apps/api/v2/src/vercel-webhook.guard.ts:53
             const isSignatureValid = crypto.timingSafeEqual(
             Real invocation. HMAC computed at L44, length pre-check at L47-L50 (which is
             required because timingSafeEqual throws on unequal lengths), call at L53-L56.
    TEXTUAL  apps/api/v2/src/vercel-webhook.guard.ts:46
             // Check signature length first (timingSafeEqual throws if lengths differ)
             A comment describing the guard on the next lines. Not executable.
    CALL     packages/app-store/_utils/oauth/decodeOAuthState.ts:30
             if (expected.length !== actual.length || !timingSafeEqual(expected, actual))
             Real invocation, with its own length pre-check in the same condition.
    TEXTUAL  packages/app-store/_utils/oauth/decodeOAuthState.ts:1
             import { createHmac, timingSafeEqual } from "node:crypto";
             A declaration that brings the primitive into scope. Not an invocation.
    CALL     packages/app-store/btcpayserver/api/webhook.ts:85
             const isValid = crypto.timingSafeEqual(
             Real invocation, call at L85-L88 between the 400 branches at L83 and L89.
  CORRECTED AUTHORITY. Constant-time comparison in first-party non-test code is present at
  exactly THREE call sites across THREE files, in the two API surfaces and one app-store
  webhook handler. The inventory correctly carries FIVE category 3 rows across those same
  three files. Both numbers are true; only the second is a recall statement and only the
  first may be used as a control-coverage statement. Every mitigation claim, every
  sink-to-mitigation ratio and every demotion reason that leans on constant-time
  comparison is based on the three invocations.
  RATIO RESTATED. The auth-decision exposure ratio must therefore be read as 134 category
  4 sink rows against 3 timing-safe INVOCATIONS - not against 5 rows. The ratio is 44.7:1,
  not 26.8:1; the corrected figure is worse, not better, so the previously published
  figure understated the exposure it was cited to demonstrate.
  SCOPE OF THE CERTIFICATION, STATED HONESTLY. Row-by-row certification was performed for
  category 3 only, because category 3 is the only mitigation category the Layer 3a
  contract pins to an exact count. For the other eight categories the CODE column above is
  an UPPER BOUND on the call-site count and is NOT asserted to equal it: a CODE row may be
  an identifier in a property position, a type annotation, or an object key rather than an
  invocation. No control-coverage claim anywhere in this assessment is derived from a raw
  CODE count for categories 1, 2 and 4 through 9.
  DOWNSTREAM RECONCILIATION. Every finding that cites constant-time comparison was
  re-read against the three certified call sites. No finding in any layer asserts a
  five-call-site count. The Layer 1 category 6 records concern the CONVERSE condition -
  comparisons that are NOT constant time - and cite their own sink lines, so the
  correction does not touch them. The Layer 3b records that credit constant-time
  comparison as a mitigation cite decodeOAuthState.ts and btcpayserver/api/webhook.ts,
  both certified CALL above, so each demotion rests on a real invocation. Both citations
  were re-verified against the source line by line, and both are exact:
    decodeOAuthState.ts:26-32 - the cited range is the whole control, not just the call:
      HMAC derivation at L26-28, the decoded comparand at L29, the constant-time guard at
      L30, and its reject-by-returning-undefined branch at L31-32.
    btcpayserver/api/webhook.ts:85-88 with the length pre-check at L82 - both offsets are
      correct: candidate signature derived at L80, length pre-check at L82 rejecting with
      400 at L83, constant-time call at L85-88, and the negative branch rejecting with 400
      at L89.
  No downstream citation required correction. That is the informative outcome: the
  overcount was confined to how the ROW COUNT was narrated in this provenance file, and it
  never propagated into a finding.
  CROSS-METHOD AGREEMENT. This classification independently reproduces the same 3 / 1 / 1
  split reached by reading the three source files directly. Two unrelated methods - a
  text-only classifier and manual source inspection - agreeing on a five-row population
  is what raises confidence that the corrected authority is right.
