# =============================================================================
# LAYER 4 - OBLIGATIONS THE NORMALIZER MUST DISCHARGE
# The raw artifact is preserved UNMODIFIED per practice E4, so every one of the
# following transformations belongs to security-audit/findings-layer-4-osv.json.
# Each is stated with the measurement that makes it actionable rather than as a
# general warning, because a warning without a target is not verifiable.
# =============================================================================
#
# --- OBLIGATION 1. RELATIVIZE THE SOURCE PATH. DISCHARGED AT THE ARTIFACT. ----
# STATUS: ALREADY DONE. This obligation is NOT deferred to the normalizer; it was
# applied to the published artifact itself. See LAYER 4 - SOURCE-PATH CANONICALIZATION
# below for the full evidence, and note that this is the ONE place where practice E4's
# preserve-unmodified default is deliberately overridden - the AAP REPRODUCIBILITY
# mandate outranks it, because the emitted value is host-specific.
# osv-scanner ALWAYS ABSOLUTIZES its source path, even when handed a relative
# lockfile argument, which is exactly what happened here: the argument was the
# relative string 'yarn.lock' and the emitted document nevertheless records an
# absolute, machine-specific path. Left unrelativized, EVERY Layer 4 finding would
# carry a path that is unique to this checkout, which breaks reproducibility and
# invalidates every baseline diff.
#   field rewritten  : results[*].source.path
#   emitted value    : <absolute path of the scanning checkout>/yarn.lock
#   published value  : yarn.lock
# BLAST RADIUS IS EXACTLY ONE FIELD. A recursive JSON-path census of the entire
# payload finds exactly ONE real filesystem path in the document, and it is that
# field. The other 53 occurrences of the temporary-directory path prefix are all inside
#   .... RETAINED PARALLEL ACCOUNT (identical path-hygiene redaction, different wording). Superseded as a live entry by the
#   .... resolution above; kept verbatim because it carries evidence the live entry
#   .... does not restate. Nothing below is an active field value.
#   field. The other 53 occurrences of the temp-directory prefix are all inside
#   .... end retained parallel account
# results[*].packages[*].vulnerabilities[*].details - upstream advisory PROSE, which
# must not be altered. The package entries, aliases and CWE lists are path-free. So
# this was a single, testable rewrite, not an open-ended audit of the document.
# ONE FALSE-POSITIVE TRAP, recorded so a future sweep does not re-litigate it and
# does not "fix" something that is not broken. Searching this document for the Linux
# user-home path prefix returns SIX hits, none of which is a host path and none of
# which is in a URL:
#   .... RETAINED PARALLEL ACCOUNT (identical path-hygiene redaction, different wording). Superseded as a live entry by the
#   .... resolution above; kept verbatim because it carries evidence the live entry
#   .... does not restate. Nothing below is an active field value.
#   does not "fix" something that is not broken. Searching this document for the
#   Linux home prefix returns SIX hits, none a host path and none inside a URL:
#   .... end retained parallel account
# they are inside upstream ADVISORY PROSE - reproduction instructions in OSV
# records, for example a DOMPurify proof-of-concept whose text includes a
# 'node <user-home-path>/<file>-poc.js' command line. That prose is vulnerability-database
#   .... RETAINED PARALLEL ACCOUNT (identical path-hygiene redaction, different wording). Superseded as a live entry by the
#   .... resolution above; kept verbatim because it carries evidence the live entry
#   .... does not restate. Nothing below is an active field value.
#   proof-of-concept command line that names a script beneath a Linux home
#   directory. That prose is vulnerability-database
#   .... end retained parallel account
# content authored by third parties and preserved verbatim, so it is neither a leak
# of this host nor something the relativization obligation should touch. The
# distinction that matters: 'absolute path OF THIS CHECKOUT' is what must be
# rewritten, and there is exactly one of those.
# This mirrors the identical trap documented for Layer 2, where four user-home-prefix
# substrings turned out to be upstream rule-documentation URLs. In both layers the
#   .... RETAINED PARALLEL ACCOUNT (identical path-hygiene redaction, different wording). Superseded as a live entry by the
#   .... resolution above; kept verbatim because it carries evidence the live entry
#   .... does not restate. Nothing below is an active field value.
#   This mirrors the identical trap documented for Layer 2, where four Linux-home-
#   prefix substrings turned out to be upstream rule-documentation URLs. In both layers the
#   .... end retained parallel account
# rule is the same - investigate every hit before either accepting or editing it.
# WHY THE PREFIXES ARE NAMED IN PROSE AND NEVER WRITTEN AS LITERALS ANYWHERE IN THIS
# FILE: the path-hygiene contract is enforced mechanically, by grepping each published
# artifact for the three host-path prefixes. A self-referential mention of a prefix -
# even one whose only purpose is to assert that the prefix is absent - trips that grep
# and is indistinguishable from a real leak. Prose therefore names them descriptively
# (temporary-directory prefix, Linux user-home prefix, macOS user-home prefix) so the
# assertion stays verifiable. This applies to audit-authored text only; the raw
# scanner documents keep their third-party prose verbatim and are never rewritten.
#
# --- OBLIGATION 2. SEVERITY IS NOT NUMERIC. Derive it; do not read it. --------
# vulnerabilities[*].severity is an ARRAY OF CVSS VECTOR STRINGS, not a number:
#   [{"score":"CVSS:3.1/AV:N/AC:L/PR:L/UI:N/S:U/C:N/I:N/A:L","type":"CVSS_V3"},
#    {"score":"CVSS:4.0/AV:N/AC:L/AT:N/PR:L/UI:N/VC:N/VI:N/VA:L/...","type":"CVSS_V4"}]
# The ONLY numeric value anywhere in the document is groups[*].max_severity, and it
# is a per-GROUP maximum, NOT a per-vulnerability score. GROUP-TO-VULNERABILITY
# ATTRIBUTION IS THEREFORE MANDATORY and it IS recoverable from the raw document,
# because groups[*].ids enumerates the advisory ids each group covers. Join on that
# array; do not assume group order matches vulnerability order.
# Measured three-step fallback chain over the 253 (package, advisory) pairs:
#   1. groups[*].max_severity                      250 records
#   2. database_specific.severity  (LOW|MODERATE|HIGH|CRITICAL)   2 records
#   3. unknown - no numeric score and no label      1 record
# Over the 259 CVE-keyed records the EMITTED artifact carries, the same chain reads
# 256 / 2 / 1: the six split records are all group-maximum-derived, so only step 1
# moves. Both denominators are recorded in the Directive 6 totals above.
# The two step-2 records are named explicitly so the fallback is verifiable:
#   cookie      GHSA-pxg6-pf52-xh8x   database_specific.severity = LOW
#   dompurify   GHSA-x4vx-rjvf-j5p4   database_specific.severity = LOW
# Note that database_specific.severity uses the GitHub vocabulary, whose MODERATE
# has no counterpart in the audit's unified critical/high/medium/low vocabulary and
# must be mapped to 'medium'. Neither step-2 record is MODERATE, so the mapping is
# not exercised on this run - but it must still be implemented, because a database
# refresh can move a record into step 2 at any time.
# CVSS bands applied to the step-1 numeric value (corroborated across five
# independent published sources): none 0.0 | low 0.1-3.9 | medium 4.0-6.9 |
# high 7.0-8.9 | critical 9.0-10.0.
#
# --- OBLIGATION 3. THE CWE JOIN KEY (practice E2) ----------------------------
#   field: vulnerabilities[*].database_specific.cwe_ids  e.g. ["CWE-400"]
# Resolved on 252 of the 253 (package, advisory) pairs - 258 of the 259 CVE-keyed
# records the artifact emits; 91 distinct CWEs appear across all candidate lists. This
# is the clean join key and no inference from the description is required for any of
# them. Every finding must carry a CWE (E2), and the mandatory field may NOT be left
# empty, so the ONE record the database leaves unclassified is populated by an
# evidence-supported inference whose provenance is disclosed per record - see
# Obligation 5 and the OBLIGATION 3 discharge section further below.
#
# --- OBLIGATION 4. DEDUPLICATION, AND THE ONE PLACE THE STATED KEY IS UNDECIDABLE
# Directive 6 says, verbatim, to deduplicate by (package_name, CVE_ID). Applied
# literally that key is UNDECIDABLE for 30 of the 370 raw records, because those
# records carry NO CVE alias at all - the advisory has only its GHSA (or MAL)
# identifier. Reporting both readings, because the difference is real and a
# downstream count that does not reconcile will fail verification check 11:
#   (package_name, advisory_id) throughout ........................ 253 records
#   (package_name, CVE_ID), falling back to the advisory id when no
#   CVE alias exists (229 CVE-keyed pairs + 30 alias-free records) . 259 records
# The 6-record difference is fully accounted for: exactly six advisories carry TWO
# CVE aliases each, so expanding on CVE splits each of them into two pairs -
#   lodash GHSA-f23m-r3pf-42rh, lodash GHSA-r5fr-rjxr-66jc,
#   lodash-es GHSA-f23m-r3pf-42rh, lodash-es GHSA-r5fr-rjxr-66jc,
#   path-to-regexp GHSA-37ch-88jc-xwx2, uuid GHSA-w5hq-g745-h8pq.
#   253 + 6 = 259, reconciling exactly.
# KEY IMPLEMENTED: (package_name, CVE_ID) - Directive 6's key verbatim - with the
# advisory id used ONLY for the 30 records that have no CVE alias, which is the sole
# place the stated key is undecidable. It yields 259 records.
# THIS SUPERSEDES AN EARLIER RECOMMENDATION IN THIS FILE, and the reversal is recorded
# rather than quietly overwritten. The earlier revision preferred (package_name,
# advisory_id) because it is total and stable under a database refresh, and emitted 253
# records. Code review established that the advisory reading DROPS a required identity:
# the second CVE of each two-CVE advisory survived only as a secondary alias inside a
# record keyed to a different CVE, so six mandated package+CVE pairs -
#   lodash|CVE-2026-4800, lodash-es|CVE-2026-4800, path-to-regexp|CVE-2026-4867,
#   uuid|CVE-2026-41988, lodash|CVE-2026-2950, lodash-es|CVE-2026-2950
# - had no record of their own. The directive's key is authoritative over a convenience
# argument, so the CVE key is implemented and the trade-offs it carries are stated here
# instead of being hidden:
#   (a) two records can share one advisory. They are NOT duplicates - they are two CVE
#       identities of one advisory - and each such record now declares the other in the
#       additive cve_alias_siblings field, so the relationship is machine-readable.
#   (b) dedup_collapsed still counts the RAW vulnerability objects behind a record, so
#       summed over the 259 records it is 379 rather than 370: the six two-CVE
#       advisories contribute their raw objects (1+1+1+4+1+1 = 9) a second time under
#       their second CVE identity. 370 + 9 = 379, reconciling exactly.
#   (c) a database refresh that adds a CVE alias to an existing advisory will add a
#       record. That is a true change in identity coverage, and the baseline diff should
#       surface it rather than absorb it.
# The key is stated inside the findings artifact on every record (dedup_key) and in the
# layer_4_normalized_dedup_key field above, so the merged summary reconciles.
# Note also WHY 370 raw records collapse at all - to 253 (package, advisory) groups
# and 259 CVE-keyed findings: 29 package NAMES appear at more than one version in the
# lockfile (fast-xml-parser at 4.4.1 and 5.3.5, @opentelemetry/core at four versions,
# ajv at four, and so on), and the same advisory is reported against each affected
# version. Version multiplicity is therefore collapsed under BOTH readings - the
# versions field retains every affected version - while CVE multiplicity is collapsed
# under the advisory reading only, which is precisely the identity loss corrected here.
#   .... RETAINED PARALLEL ACCOUNT (same reversal to the directive key, without the 379 reconciliation). Superseded as a live entry by the
#   .... resolution above; kept verbatim because it carries evidence the live entry
#   .... does not restate. Nothing below is an active field value.
#   KEY ACTUALLY USED: the directive's literal key, (package_name, CVE_ID), expanded to
#   CVE granularity, with the advisory id standing in for the 30 alias-free records. It
#   yields 259 findings. An earlier revision of this file RECOMMENDED
#   (package_name, advisory_id) instead, on the grounds that it is total, that it is
#   stable when a database refresh adds a CVE alias to an existing advisory, that it
#   never double-counts an advisory carrying two CVE numbers, and that it yields 253 -
#   the figure the plan measured. THAT RECOMMENDATION IS WITHDRAWN. It optimises the
#   wrong property: collapsing to the advisory DROPS six package/CVE identities that the
#   database publishes, so a consumer searching the audit for CVE-2026-2950,
#   CVE-2026-4800, CVE-2026-4867 or CVE-2026-41988 would find nothing. Losing a
#   published identifier is a recall defect and is worse than emitting one advisory under
#   both of its identifiers, which is disclosed per record by the additive
#   cve_co_assigned flag. The advisory-level count is preserved and remains queryable as
#   the 253 distinct (package, advisory) pairs. See the normalized-artifact section below
#   for the corrected key, its counts and its reconciliation identity.
#   Note also WHY 370 raw records collapse to 253: 29 package NAMES appear at more
#   than one version in the lockfile (fast-xml-parser at 4.4.1 and 5.3.5,
#   @opentelemetry/core at four versions, ajv at four, and so on), and the same
#   advisory is reported against each affected version.
#   .... end retained parallel account
#
# --- OBLIGATION 5. HONEST COVERAGE - the single degenerate record (practice E8)
# One record has NO CVE alias, NO cwe_ids, NO CVSS vector array and an empty
# groups[0].max_severity. It must be REPORTED, not hidden:
#   package  http @ 0.0.1-security (npm)
#   advisory MAL-2025-22760   summary: "Malicious code in http (npm)"
#   severity null | aliases absent | database_specific {"malicious-packages-origins": null}
# Two facts make it materially more interesting than its missing metadata suggests.
# First, it is a MALICIOUS-PACKAGE advisory, a class the repository's current gate
# cannot express at all. Second, http@0.0.1-security is a DIRECT PRODUCTION
# dependency of @calcom/api-proxy - declared in apps/api/package.json alongside
# connect 3.7.0 and http-proxy-middleware 2.0.9 - and NOT a transitive. Whatever
# severity and CWE the normalizer assigns must be justified in the finding text
# rather than inferred silently, and 'unknown' must not be quietly rounded to 'low'.
# HOW IT IS DISCHARGED, both halves stated because they are treated differently:
#   severity - remains the FLOOR of the closed vocabulary, and severity_basis says so
#              in words ("UNDERIVABLE ... NOT derived; impact is not bounded by this
#              value"). Nothing is rounded and nothing is claimed.
#   cwe      - is POPULATED with CWE-506 Embedded Malicious Code, because the schema
#              field is mandatory and an empty string is a schema violation rather than
#              an act of honesty. The classification is justified by the advisory's own
#              text ("The package http was found to contain malicious code"), the record
#              keeps cwe_resolved: false so the OSV database is not credited with it,
#              and the additive cwe_basis field states the inference verbatim on the
#              record itself. The finding's description also names the inference, so a
#              reader of the artifact alone cannot mistake it for database metadata.
#
# --- OBLIGATION 6. THE VERSION-OVERRIDE CAVEAT, MADE DECIDABLE ---------------
# The root package.json declares 41 resolutions entries covering 37 distinct
# override targets. A resolutions override means the version RECORDED in the
# lockfile entry and the version EFFECTIVELY INSTALLED can differ, so an OSV match
# is evidence of a POSSIBLE exposure rather than proof of a live one. Every Layer 4
# finding must note whether an override applies to its package. Rather than leave
# that as a blanket warning, the intersection was measured:
#   12 of the 75 OSV-flagged package names have a root override, and 66 of the 370
#   raw vulnerability records sit on one:
#     axios (1.13.5), form-data (4.0.4), js-yaml (4.1.1), lodash (4.17.23),
#     lodash-es (4.17.23), node-forge (1.3.2), qs (6.14.1), rollup (4.22.4),
#     serialize-javascript (6.0.2), tar (7.5.7), typeorm (0.3.27),
#     fast-xml-parser (4.4.1 and 5.3.5)
#   For ELEVEN of those twelve the flagged lockfile version is IDENTICAL to the
#   override target, so for those the caveat resolves to 'no divergence - the
#   override is already materialized in the lockfile and the match is against the
#   overridden version'. That is a stronger statement than the generic caveat, and
#   it means those findings are NOT weakened by the override.
#   fast-xml-parser is the ONE exception and it inverts the caveat - see below.
#   The remaining 25 override targets are not flagged by OSV at all.
#
# --- OBLIGATION 7. THE SUPPRESSION-BOUNDARY FINDING -------------------------
# .yarnrc.yml:L7-L11 suppresses ONE npm advisory, npmAuditIgnoreAdvisories:
# ["1113407"], for an XML parser. It does so CORRECTLY BY THE REPOSITORY'S OWN
# STANDARD and that must be CREDITED, not criticised: the entry carries a
# three-line written justification at .yarnrc.yml:L7-L9 stating that the parser
# only handles trusted AWS API responses rather than user input, that there is no
# practical attack vector, and that an upstream fix is pending. That is good
# practice, and this assessment applies the same standard in reverse - every
# demotion it makes carries a reason of comparable specificity.
# The problem is the BOUNDARY of the suppression, not its quality. It binds ONLY
# the Yarn auditor. Measured against the independent OSV database:
#   yarn.lock:L22747 and L22749 still record fast-xml-parser@npm:4.4.1, and the
#   scan returns SIX advisories against that version -
#     GHSA-8gc5-j5rx-235r  CVE-2026-33036  CWE-776   7.5
#     GHSA-fj3w-jwp8-x2g3  CVE-2026-27942  CWE-120   2.7
#     GHSA-gh4j-gqv2-49f6  CVE-2026-41650  CWE-91    6.1
#     GHSA-jmr7-xgp7-cmfj  CVE-2026-26278  CWE-776   7.5
#     GHSA-jp2q-39xq-3w4g  CVE-2026-33349  CWE-1284  5.9
#     GHSA-m7jm-9gc2-mpf2  CVE-2026-25896  CWE-185   9.3  <- CRITICAL
#   The written justification addresses ONE of them. Five, including the 9.3
#   critical, are neither suppressed nor addressed.
# Three further measurements complete the finding:
#   (a) The existing CI gate blocks only at critical severity, and it does so VIA
#       THE VERY AUDITOR THE SUPPRESSION SILENCES -
#       .github/workflows/security-audit.yml:L19 runs
#       'yarn npm audit --all --recursive --severity critical'.
#   (b) The resolutions pin to 5.3.5 does NOT remediate. The scan of 5.3.5 returns
#       FIVE of the same six advisories - every one except the 9.3 critical
#       GHSA-m7jm-9gc2-mpf2. Independently reconfirmed on this run.
#   (c) The pin does not even reach the affected instance. The override key is
#       SCOPED: "@lingo.dev/_compiler/fast-xml-parser": "5.3.5". It governs only
#       that one parent, so it does not apply to the @boxyhq/saml-jackson ->
#       @aws-sdk/core transitive that pulls in 4.4.1, and BOTH 4.4.1 and 5.3.5
#       remain present in the lockfile. This is the one case where the override
#       caveat inverts: the override does not weaken the finding, it fails to
#       cover it.
# This is the clearest available demonstration of why Layer 4 uses an independent
# vulnerability database rather than the package manager's own auditor.
#
# --- OBLIGATION 8. MUTABLE VERSION SPECIFIERS -------------------------------
# Two exist across the whole monorepo, and the second is materially worse:
#   checkly: "latest"        root devDependencies      - CI-only blast radius
#   @snyk/protect: "latest"  apps/api/v2/package.json:L64, inside the PRODUCTION
#                            dependencies block - a production runtime dependency
#                            whose resolved code can change with NO manifest change,
#                            which by construction no lockfile-based scan can bound.
#
# --- OBLIGATION 9. PER-SURFACE VERSION SKEW ---------------------------------
# An advisory can apply to one surface and not another, so findings must not be
# collapsed across surfaces:
#   stripe   9.16.0  in apps/web        vs  15.4.0 in apps/api/v2 - two majors run
#                                           simultaneously, so webhook-signature
#                                           semantics differ per surface
#   next     16.1.7  in apps/web        vs  16.1.5 in apps/api/v1 - patch skew, so a
#                                           patch-level advisory can hit one only
#   engines.yarn "3.4.1" is declared in apps/web/package.json against the root
#   packageManager pin "yarn@4.12.0". The root pin governs, so this is stale
#   manifest drift rather than a blocker - but it is exactly the kind of drift an
#   audit should surface.
# node-ical 0.16.1 is declared at root package.json:L110, which confirms the XXE
# and deserialization sink categories have real substrate rather than being
# theoretical for this codebase.
#
# --- OBLIGATION 10. THE MERGE MUST DEDUPLICATE INTER-LAYER ONLY (A8) --------
# There is exactly ONE lockfile, so results[*].source.path is a single value and
# EVERY Layer 4 finding keys on the SAME path. Directive 7 also mandates line = 0
# for every dependency finding. File and line are therefore CONSTANT across all
# Layer 4 findings, and a merge key of file+line+CWE collapses them purely by CWE.
# With 80 distinct chosen CWEs over 259 records that is catastrophic over-collapse, and it
# manufactures corroborations that do not exist. THE MERGE MUST DEDUPLICATE
# INTER-LAYER ONLY: two findings originating in the same layer must never merge.
#
# --- OBLIGATION 11. THE EXISTING-TOOLING DELTA ------------------------------
# The repository's current dependency gate is the Yarn auditor at severity
# 'critical' (.github/workflows/security-audit.yml:L19), not OSV. There is no
# CodeQL, Snyk, Trivy, Semgrep or Gitleaks workflow anywhere in .github/workflows,
# and no .github/dependabot.yml exists - both verified by direct inspection. OSV is
# strictly broader in database coverage and, decisively, is NOT subject to the
# repository's Yarn-specific advisory suppression. Nothing here is wired into CI:
# .github/workflows/security-audit.yml is read as evidence and never edited, and no
# task is added to turbo.json.
#
# =============================================================================
# LAYER 4 - WHAT THIS LAYER MUST *NOT* BE USED FOR
# Stated explicitly because the failure mode is easy to reach by accident and the
# consequence is a gate that is useless in practice.
# =============================================================================
# LAYER 4 SEVERITY MUST NOT DRIVE THE GATE VERDICT. This layer reports 10 critical
# and 106 high findings. A gate keyed on that severity distribution would BLOCK on
# essentially every scan of essentially every npm project, and a gate that always
# blocks is indistinguishable from no gate at all. The published distribution of npm
# advisories makes this concrete: across 1498 npm vulnerabilities studied, 0.53% were
# low, 31.11% medium, 42.06% high and 26.30% critical - so high-and-above is the
# ordinary case, not the exceptional one.
# The verdict is instead keyed on EXPLOITABILITY, established by Layer 3b, and it is
# computed exclusively from the requirements' own truth table in strict
# ERROR > BLOCK > WARN > PASS precedence. NO threshold, service level or key
# indicator is invented here, and none may be inferred from these counts: the
# repository declares no numeric security SLA, KPI or coverage threshold anywhere,
# and this assessment does not supply one. The only numeric mapping this layer
# applies is the standard CVSS band table above, which is a published external
# standard rather than a locally chosen cut-off.
# The reason exploitability rather than severity is the right key is well evidenced:
# most dependency alerts are not reachable at all. 70-80% of declared dependencies
# are never referenced; one industry analysis found the flagged dependency unused in
# 84.2% of alerts; a large Maven study found reachable call paths for under 1% of
# packages; and a conservative call-graph reachability filter cut 121 reported
# vulnerabilities to 31 across twelve Go projects, a 74.4% false-positive reduction.
# Only about 32% of automated remediation pull requests are ever merged.
# CONSEQUENCE FOR THIS LAYER'S CONTRIBUTION TO THE GATE: Layer 4 contributes
# FINDINGS and its OK/ERROR status, and nothing else. Its status can raise the
# verdict to ERROR - but only by failing to produce a parseable document, never by
# finding vulnerabilities. Its findings feed the total that the WARN branch compares
# against the baseline. Its severities never, on their own, produce BLOCK.
#
# =============================================================================
# LAYER 4 - COVERAGE AND HONEST DISCLOSURE (practice E8)
# =============================================================================
# LOCKFILE COVERAGE IS COMPLETE FOR EVERY LOCKFILE THE SCANNER CAN PARSE.
# codebase-profile.txt records lockfiles: yarn.lock, and an independent recursive
# sweep over nine lockfile names outside node_modules/.yarn/.git confirms ./yarn.lock
# is the only match. One further git-tracked lockfile exists that Directive 0's
# root-level 'ls' cannot see:
#   packages/embeds/embed-core/bun.lockb - 82677 bytes of BINARY Bun lockfile format
# The pinned scanner REJECTS it, re-verified on this run:
#   "could not determine extractor suitable to this file", 0 Extract calls
# No text bun.lock exists anywhere in the tree, so there is no parseable substitute.
# It is therefore NOT a Layer 4 target and the profile's field value stands. It is
# recorded here for two reasons: so no downstream layer concludes the sweep missed a
# lockfile, and because a stale binary lockfile committed inside a published package
# is itself an observation worth carrying forward.
#
# DELTAS AGAINST THE PLAN'S MEASUREMENTS, DISCLOSED RATHER THAN SMOOTHED.
# The plan's Layer 4 figures were measured earlier against the SAME live OSV
# database, which moves as advisories are published and amended. Reconciliation:
#   deduplicated record count   plan ~253   measured 253 on the (package, advisory)
#                               reading; 259 EMITTED on Directive 6's (package, CVE)
#                               key, which is within the plan's own '~253' wording and
#                               is the key the directive states verbatim
#   severity source chain       plan 250 / 2 / 1           measured 250 / 2 / 1 on the
#                               advisory reading; 256 / 2 / 1 as emitted, the six split
#                               records all taking a group maximum
#   critical / high / medium    plan 10 / 102 / 116        measured 10 / 102 / 116 on the
#                               advisory reading; 10 / 106 / 118 as emitted
#   low                         plan 25                    measured 24 low + 1 unknown
#                               (25 emitted, the unknown carrying the vocabulary floor)
#   CWE resolved                plan 251 of 253            measured 252 of 253 from OSV
#                               metadata; as emitted, 258 of 259 from metadata plus 1
#                               inferred, so 259 of 259 populated and 0 empty
# The severity rows are the same single record accounted differently: the degenerate
# EVERY FIGURE IN THAT TABLE IS AT ADVISORY LEVEL - the denominator is the 253
# (package, advisory) pairs, which is what the plan measured and what this raw census
# counts. The PUBLISHED artifact is keyed per CVE, as Directive 6 literally requires, so
# its own census is 259 findings with 10 critical / 106 high / 118 medium / 25 low and
# CWE resolved on 258 of 259. The two are the same data under two denominators, and the
# difference is exactly the six advisories that publish two CVE aliases each. Both are
# stated so that neither reads as drift; see the normalized-artifact section below.
# MAL-2025-22760 entry described in Obligation 5 is reported here as 'unknown' rather
# than folded into 'low', because inventing a severity for a record that carries none
# would be a fabrication - its emitted severity is the vocabulary FLOOR and its
# severity_basis says in words that the value is not derived. Its CWE is the one field
# treated differently, and deliberately: the schema makes cwe mandatory, so leaving it
# empty is a schema violation rather than restraint, and it is populated by inference
# with the provenance disclosed on the record. The measured values are reported AS
# MEASURED. No figure is adjusted to match the plan.
# SCOPE NOTE, so this table is not misread: every figure above is a census of the
# RAW OSV document, which is never modified. In the NORMALIZED artifact the same
# record is severity 'low' (the closed vocabulary has no 'unknown' member, and the
# coercion is recorded as normalized_severity_floor_coercions) and its CWE is
# CWE-506 by derivation (recorded as layer_4_cwe_derivation). The raw census and
# the normalized census differ on exactly this one record, by design, and both are
# stated rather than reconciled into a single misleading number.
# SCOPE NOTE, so this table is not misread: every figure above is a census of the
# RAW OSV document, which is never modified. In the NORMALIZED artifact the same
# record is severity 'low' (the closed vocabulary has no 'unknown' member, and the
# coercion is recorded as normalized_severity_floor_coercions) and its CWE is
# CWE-506 by derivation (recorded as layer_4_cwe_derivation). The raw census and
# the normalized census differ on exactly this one record, by design, and both are
# stated rather than reconciled into a single misleading number.
#
# =============================================================================
# LAYER 4 - REPRODUCIBILITY EVIDENCE
# =============================================================================
# The reproducibility mandate binds the deterministic layers - 0, 2, 3a and 4 -
# to produce identical output from identical input. Demonstrated, not asserted:
#   Independent invocations across two command forms all produced a byte-identical
#   scanner payload, and cmp reports byte-identity in every pairing. The payload's own
#   byte count and digest are deliberately not quoted here, because the scanner embeds
#   the absolute path of whichever checkout ran it and both figures are therefore
#   host-specific; the reproducible quantities are the published artifact's, below.
# The single host-dependent element in the scanner's own output is
# results[*].source.path, the absolutized path of Obligation 1. It is CANONICALIZED to
# the repository-relative 'yarn.lock' in the published artifact, so the PUBLISHED
# document is byte-reproducible ACROSS CHECKOUTS and not merely within one. The
# canonicalizing transform is itself deterministic and idempotent: applied to either
# of two byte-identical scanner outputs it yields byte-identical results, and applied
# to an already-relative path it is a fixed point.
#   scanner stdout  host-specific byte count and digest (absolute path embedded)
#   published       2828403 bytes  sha256 1e78d81a...e82ef3ed5d (relative path)
#   The two differ by exactly one field and by exactly the length of the path removed,
#   which is what makes the published figure the one worth recording.
# The ANSI-stripping pipeline stage (IR4) is byte-transparent on this payload -
# stripped and unstripped captures share the same md5 - which is the desired result:
# the guarantee holds because it is enforced on capture, not because the scanner
# happened to emit clean output. Measured 0 escape bytes and 0 carriage returns.
# Offline re-run flags, noted for an air-gapped repeat (IR7): --offline,
# --offline-vulnerabilities, --download-offline-databases. The default package
# information source is deps.dev; network egress is required for a normal run and
# was available (api.osv.dev/v1/query returned HTTP 200).
#
# =============================================================================
# LAYER 4 - THE TWO TRAPS, RECORDED SO THEY ARE NOT RE-SPRUNG
# =============================================================================
# IR10 - EXIT CODES ARE NOT FAILURE. osv-scanner EXITS 1 WHEN IT FINDS
# VULNERABILITIES, which is the EXPECTED outcome for this repository, and it is what
# happened: exit code 1 with a fully parseable document (published at 2828403 bytes
# after source-path canonicalization).
# layer_4_status therefore keys on "the result document was produced AND parses",
# NEVER on the exit code, and is recorded as OK. Keying on the exit code would mark
# a SUCCESSFUL layer as ERROR, and because the gate's ERROR branch fires on any
# layer_N_status of ERROR and ERROR outranks BLOCK, WARN and PASS in strict
# precedence, that single mistake would wrongly poison the entire verdict.
#
# DIRECTIVE 6'S COMMAND IS NOT DEFECTIVE - DO NOT "FIX" IT. Unlike Directive 2,
# whose two commands genuinely do not execute, Directive 6's legacy invocation form
# still works in 2.4.0. Verified on this run: the verbatim legacy form
#   osv-scanner --lockfile=yarn.lock --format json
# and the modern subcommand form
#   osv-scanner scan source --lockfile=yarn.lock --format json
# both exit 1 and emit BYTE-IDENTICAL payloads - same byte count, same digest, cmp
# clean - and both canonicalize to the same published artifact.
# Reconfirmed on this run in this checkout. The modern form is used here for forward
# compatibility only; the legacy form is equally valid and must not be "corrected" by a
# downstream agent.
#
# =============================================================================
# LAYER 4 - SOURCE-PATH CANONICALIZATION (review finding F1)
# =============================================================================
# WHAT THE REVIEW FOUND. The previously published results-osv.json recorded, at
# results[0].source.path, an absolute path belonging to a DIFFERENT parallel clone
# of this repository - a SIBLING PARALLEL CLONE - rather than the clone the artifact
# was actually published from. Two defects in one field: an absolute, host-specific
# path defeats
# the AAP REPRODUCIBILITY mandate and every baseline diff, and naming a sibling
# agent's working directory discloses filesystem layout that has no place in a
# published artifact. The finding is FIXED, not documented-around.
#
# WHY THE RAW DOCUMENT IS REWRITTEN AT ALL - THE PRECEDENCE RULING.
# Practice E4 preserves tool output unmodified by default, and that default holds
# everywhere else in this artifact. It is deliberately overridden for THIS ONE FIELD
# because the AAP REPRODUCIBILITY requirement ("deterministic layers 0, 2, 3a and 4
# produce identical outputs on identical inputs") cannot be satisfied while the
# document carries a path that changes with the checkout location. Preserving a value
# that is BOTH host-specific AND wrong would preserve the defect, not the evidence.
# The override is confined to one field, and the semantic-equivalence proof below is
# what makes the override auditable rather than merely asserted.
#
# THE FIX, AND HOW IT WAS APPLIED.
#   field           results[0].source.path
#   before          <absolute root of a different, sibling parallel clone>/yarn.lock
#                   (the offending value is described rather than reproduced here, so
#                   that correcting the disclosure does not re-create it)
#   after           yarn.lock
#   derivation      os.path.relpath(emitted_path, repository_root) == 'yarn.lock'
#   method          surgical replacement in the WIRE TEXT, not a re-serialization.
#                   The JSON-encoded needle was asserted to occur exactly once, then
#                   replaced once. Re-serializing through json.dumps would have
#                   rewritten the scanner's own formatting throughout the document and
#                   introduced drift far beyond the field being corrected.
#   blast radius    1 line, 1 field, -77 bytes. 'git diff -U0' on the artifact shows
#                   exactly one '-' line and one '+' line.
#
# SEMANTIC EQUIVALENCE - PROVEN, NOT ASSUMED.
# The lockfile was re-scanned in THIS clone and the result compared against the
# reviewed artifact with a recursive deep-equality test that excludes ONLY
# results[0].source.path. Result: EQUAL. The re-scan therefore changes no finding, no
# package, no advisory, no alias, no severity and no CWE - it changes provenance only.
#   deep_equal(reviewed, republished, excluding source.path) = True
#   121 vulnerable package entries   - unchanged
#   370 vulnerability records        - unchanged
#   370 groups                       - unchanged
#   250 distinct advisory ids        - unchanged
#   224 distinct CVE aliases         - unchanged
#   253 (package, advisory) pairs    - unchanged
#
# INPUT BINDING - what exactly was scanned, so the output can be re-derived.
#   lockfile        yarn.lock (repository-relative argument, as required)
#   lockfile bytes  1433240
#   lockfile sha256 dd921ea222695ff1ee112fa16e755fc0907018a62242a556d69c81b8ce57a679
#   lockfile nlink  1 (regular file, not a link; no symlink on any path component)
#   commit          a49ab0c628c6e2b844ebc753d497684aa80d3d20
#   branch          WITHHELD - ephemeral per-execution harness identifier, no bearing on
#                   the audited code; the commit above and the lockfile digest already
#                   bind the run (see layer_4_repository_branch)
#   scanner         osv-scanner 2.4.0 / osv-scalibr 0.4.5
#                   commit b56b5191101d5f27d4787d5583d8d01e9518a7af
#                   built at 2026-06-18T12:55:27Z
#   exit code       1  ->  SUCCESS, not failure (IR10; see THE TWO TRAPS above)
#   duration        1.778 s first run, 2.426 s determinism re-run
# The lockfile digest recorded here is IDENTICAL to the digest this file already
# recorded for the reviewed run, which independently confirms that the scanned input
# never changed and that the absolutized source path was a provenance error alone -
# never a difference of input.
#
# PATH HYGIENE OF THE PUBLISHED ARTIFACT - asserted, with counts.
#   absolute filesystem paths                                    0
#   occurrences of the sibling clone's directory name             0
#   occurrences of the sibling clone's unique path suffix          0
#   occurrences of the shared workspace root prefix               0
#   occurrences of the temporary-directory prefix remaining      53
#   .... RETAINED PARALLEL ACCOUNT (identical path-hygiene redaction, different wording). Superseded as a live entry by the
#   .... resolution above; kept verbatim because it carries evidence the live entry
#   .... does not restate. Nothing below is an active field value.
#     occurrences of the temp-directory prefix remaining           53
#   .... end retained parallel account
# All 53 survivors are inside results[0].packages[*].vulnerabilities[*].details, i.e.
# upstream OSV advisory PROSE authored by advisory writers and reproduced verbatim by
# the scanner. They are NOT filesystem references belonging to this host and they are
# NOT audit-authored text. Rewriting them would corrupt a third-party document and
# break the equivalence contract with the scanner's output, so they are preserved.
#
# =============================================================================
# LAYER 4 - CREDENTIAL-SHAPED STRINGS IN UPSTREAM ADVISORY PROSE (disposition)
# =============================================================================
# WHY THIS SECTION EXISTS. The same deterministic credential detector that redacted
# the Layer 2 SARIF snippet and two Layer 3a inventory lines was run recursively over
# every string in the published Layer 4 artifact. It reports ELEVEN matches. They are
# recorded here with an explicit disposition rather than silently ignored, because an
# unexplained detector hit is indistinguishable from an overlooked leak.
#
# WHERE THEY ARE - measured, and the answer is unanimous.
#   JSON-path field distribution of the 11 matches : {'details': 11}
#   matches inside results[0].packages[*].vulnerabilities[*].details : 11 of 11
#   matches anywhere else in the document                            :  0 of 11
# 'details' is the OSV schema's free-text advisory description. Every match is inside
# advisory prose. None is in a path, a package name, a version, an alias, a severity
# vector, a CWE list or any audit-authored field.
#
# WHAT THEY ARE - three packages, eleven advisories. Class and matched LENGTH only;
# no value is recorded anywhere in this artifact set (AAP 0.8.2, practice E7).
#   #   package@version      advisory              detector classes (length)
#   --------------------------------------------------------------------------------
#    1  axios@1.13.5         GHSA-35jp-ww65-95wh   password(16), password(15)
#    2  axios@1.13.5         GHSA-3g43-6gmg-66jw   secret(15), password(19), password(20)
#    3  axios@1.13.5         GHSA-3w6x-2g7m-8v23   apiKey(22), apiKey(22)
#    4  axios@1.13.5         GHSA-7q8q-rj6j-mhjq   authLeaked(31)
#    5  axios@1.13.5         GHSA-mmx7-hfxf-jppx   Authorization(25), token(6)
#    6  axios@1.13.5         GHSA-pf86-5x62-jrwf   token(12), token(12), auth(31),
#                                                  auth(33), password(7)
#    7  axios@1.13.5         GHSA-q8qp-cvcw-x6jj   password(5)
#    8  axios@1.13.5         GHSA-xx6v-rp6x-q39c   withXSRFToken(5)
#    9  nodemailer@7.0.12    GHSA-r7g4-qg5f-qqm2   access_token(14), clientSecret(22),
#                                                  refreshToken(22)
#   10  webpack@5.90.1       GHSA-38r7-794h-5758   LEAKED_SECRET(89)
#   11  webpack@5.90.1       GHSA-8fgc-7cc6-rx7x   LEAKED_SECRET(89)
# The classes are self-explaining: these are the proof-of-concept and example
# credentials that advisory authors embed in write-ups to demonstrate a leak - the
# axios cross-site-request-forgery and Authorization-header-leak advisories, the
# nodemailer OAuth2 advisory, and the two webpack advisories whose demonstration
# variable is literally named LEAKED_SECRET.
#
# PROVENANCE - PROVEN UPSTREAM, NOT REPOSITORY-DERIVED. Three independent tests:
#   (a) BYTE-IDENTITY WITH THE SCANNER'S OWN STDOUT. Every
#       vulnerabilities[*].details field in the published artifact is byte-identical to
#       the corresponding field in the raw scanner stdout, and the detector reports the
#       SAME 11 matches at the SAME JSON paths with the SAME classes and lengths in
#       both. The audit authored none of this text; osv-scanner reproduced it from the
#       OSV database.
#   (b) ABSENT FROM THE REPOSITORY. Each flagged literal was searched across every
#       tracked file with 'git grep --fixed-strings', excluding security-audit/. The
#       non-generic literals - the ones long enough to be credential-like - are present
#       in NO tracked repository file whatsoever. They exist only inside advisory prose.
#   (c) THE GENERIC RESIDUE IS PROVABLY NOT SECRET. The handful of very short matches
#       (lengths 5 and 6) are common English and identifier fragments that occur in
#       thousands of tracked files across .github/, apps/, packages/, docs/, locale
#       JSON and yarn.lock. Ubiquity at that scale is itself proof that they carry no
#       secret value; they are detector noise from a deliberately broad keyed-literal
#       class, not credentials.
#
# DISPOSITION AND THE PRECEDENCE REASONING.
#   NO REDACTION IS APPLIED to these eleven fields, and that is the correct outcome.
#   AAP 0.8.2's absolute prohibition - "No secret values, ever" - together with
#   practice E7, governs material the assessment EXTRACTS FROM THE REPOSITORY UNDER
#   TEST. That is precisely why the Layer 2 SARIF snippet and the two Layer 3a
#   inventory lines WERE redacted: those values came out of this repository's own
#   files. The eleven matches here are categorically different on every axis that
#   matters:
#     - they are third-party published documentation, not repository content;
#     - they are deliberately published demonstration values with no confidentiality
#       to protect - redacting them protects nothing;
#     - redacting them would corrupt a verbatim upstream document, destroy the
#       byte-identity relationship with the scanner's stdout, and break the
#       semantic-equivalence and determinism proofs this section rests on;
#     - they were present unchanged in the reviewed artifact and the review did not
#       raise them, so treating them as newly-introduced leakage would misreport this
#       remediation's own blast radius.
#   The canonicalization performed for F1 changed exactly one line and introduced not
#   one of these strings.
#   HONEST DISCLOSURE (practice E8). The eleven matches are disclosed here in full,
#   with package, advisory, class and length, rather than suppressed by narrowing the
#   detector until the count reached zero. A downstream reviewer can re-run the same
#   detector, obtain the same eleven paths, and check this disposition against them.
#   Any FUTURE detector hit that falls OUTSIDE
#   results[0].packages[*].vulnerabilities[*].details must be treated as a genuine
#   leak and redacted - that boundary, not the raw count, is the standing assertion.
#
# =============================================================================
# LAYER 4 - SCOPE OF THE ASSESSMENT (practices E6 and E9)
# =============================================================================
# ASSESSMENT ONLY. NOTHING WAS REMEDIATED. No 'osv-scanner fix' was run, no
# dependency was upgraded, no lockfile was regenerated, no resolutions entry was
# edited, and no advisory suppression was added or removed. package.json, the 119
# workspace manifests, .yarnrc.yml and yarn.lock were READ and never written.
# NO DEPENDENCY WAS INSTALLED. OSV-Scanner reads the lockfile, so node_modules is
# not required; the scan extracted all 3725 packages from a PRISTINE checkout with
# no node_modules present. An install would also have dirtied the working tree.
# LEAST-PRIVILEGE SCOPING (E9). Only the local source checkout was analyzed. No
# live, deployed or remote target was probed and the committed k6 load harness was
# never invoked, honouring SECURITY.md:L31-L32: "Do not run automated scanners on
# our infrastructure or dashboard." The scanner's own accounting corroborates the
# narrowness of the read - 1 inode visited, 1 Extract call - and the only network
# traffic is advisory lookups against the public OSV database.
# The repository's disclosure policy also lists classes that are out of scope for
# its bounty programme. That list informs SEVERITY and GATE-BLOCKING RATIONALE ONLY.
# It never suppresses a category the requirements mandate: a finding in a
# policy-excluded class is still recorded, it is simply unlikely to be gate-blocking.
# READ-ONLY GUARANTEE (IR12). Verified before and after this layer against the
# project baseline commit e988138b24 with 'git diff e988138b24 --name-status':
# EXACTLY ONE pre-existing tracked file is modified - .gitignore, the single
# AAP-sanctioned UPDATE of 3 insertions and 0 deletions permitted by AAP 0.6.3 - and
# ZERO application source files, manifests, lockfiles, workflows or container
# definitions are modified. Everything else in the working tree is this audit's own
# additive artifacts under security-audit/. An earlier revision of this section
# claimed "zero pre-existing tracked files modified", which was FALSE because it
# overlooked that sanctioned .gitignore UPDATE; the claim is corrected here rather
# than left to be discovered.
# =============================================================================
# CODE-REVIEW REMEDIATION PASS - SAFETY CONTROLS FOR THE AUDIT PIPELINE ITSELF
# =============================================================================
# Added by the remediation pass that resolved the eight MAJOR findings raised
# against this artifact boundary. This section records the two controls the review
# required, their exact procedures, and the adversarial evidence that each one
# fails CLOSED. Both controls protect the AUDIT PIPELINE, not the audited product;
# no application source file is touched by either of them.
#
# Finding F3 (Supply-Chain Integrity, CWE-829): .gitignore:119 ignores the whole
#   security-audit/ tree, so an untracked YAML dropped into security-audit/rules/
#   never appears in ordinary "git status" output - yet Semgrep loads that directory
#   as its configuration. A contaminated workspace could alter or deny-of-service the
#   scan invisibly. Verified: "git check-ignore -v security-audit/rules/zz.yaml"
#   reports ".gitignore:119:security-audit/", i.e. a hypothetical injected pack IS
#   hidden from status output. The hiding is real and is what CONTROL 1 closes.
# Finding F8 (Path Containment, CWE-59 + CWE-367): the same predictable, ignored tree
#   is the direct write target for scanner and download output, with no no-follow path
#   checks and no atomic replacement, so a pre-positioned or swapped symlink/hardlink
#   could redirect a write outside security-audit/. CONTROL 2 closes that.

# ---- Why .gitignore itself is NOT edited (stated so it is not "fixed" later) ---
# Both findings are LOCATED at .gitignore:119 because that is where the hiding
# originates, but neither resolution asks for an ignore-rule change, and the plan
# forbids one. The plan freezes this file's change to exactly three appended lines:
# do NOT add extra entries such as "security-audit/**", "!security-audit/verify.sh",
# or per-file negations, because git's directory-ignore covers the whole subtree and
# the plan specifies exactly one entry. Its acceptance contract is likewise exact -
# 119 lines, 3 insertions / 0 deletions against the baseline commit, and
# "git check-ignore -v security-audit/" matching ".gitignore:119:security-audit/".
# All three were re-verified during remediation and .gitignore is byte-unchanged.
# The residual risk the ignore rule creates is therefore closed where the review
# actually asked for it to be closed: at the load step and at the write step.
gitignore_lines: 119
gitignore_diff_against_baseline: 3 insertions, 0 deletions (additive only)
gitignore_modified_by_remediation: NO - byte-identical, plan-frozen
env_template_negations_intact: YES - .env.example and .env.appStore.example remain
  un-ignored and therefore tracked, which is what makes secret-value redaction
  mandatory rather than stylistic (practice E7)

# =============================================================================
# CONTROL 1 (F3) - FAIL-CLOSED RULE-PACK INTEGRITY GATE
# =============================================================================
# Contract: before EVERY --validate and before EVERY scan, prove that the
# configuration directory contains EXACTLY the three allowlisted regular files at
# their trusted digests. Directory discovery is never trusted on its own again.
#
# WHY THE GATE RATHER THAN PER-FILE --config: the review's wording "load only that
# immutable allowlist rather than trusting directory discovery" is satisfied by
# proving the directory IS the allowlist immediately before load. Passing the three
# packs as three separate --config file arguments is NOT available: IR9 is a binding
# plan decision that Semgrep must be invoked with the RELATIVE DIRECTORY path
# security-audit/rules, because Semgrep derives every rule identifier from the
# --config path. Changing the path form rewrites all 725 rule identifiers and with
# them the SARIF severity join, the cross-layer deduplication key and every baseline
# diff. The gate achieves the same guarantee without breaking that contract.

rules_allowlist_entry_count: 3 (exact - not a minimum)
rules_allowlist_secrets.yaml: sha256 139b35ad3442bc83d1f0864db82fa4fdc7e1f1ee4b5ac872bfbeb604c82c6518, 89772 bytes
rules_allowlist_security-audit.yaml: sha256 b109a039df712f30c6d3e25e1e8358053fd0f1c91b92d0e8d2871cd141fe602f, 473625 bytes
rules_allowlist_owasp-top-ten.yaml: sha256 f0a4662179ed026409026367c8942e8a5c6da19de0ebfb8fe4127526535cd317, 1448110 bytes
rules_allowlist_digest_source: SECTION 8 pack_1/2/3_raw_sha256, re-measured live during
  remediation and matching on all three packs

# GATE PROCEDURE (deterministic, re-runnable, in order; any failure aborts the run):
#   1. Walk every path component of security-audit/rules from the pinned repository
#      root downwards using lstat, so a symlink is DETECTED rather than followed.
#      Refuse if any component is a symlink or is not a real directory.
#   2. List the directory and require the sorted listing to EQUAL the allowlist -
#      an extra entry and a missing entry are both refusals, reported with the exact
#      unexpected/missing names.
#   3. For each allowlisted name: refuse unless it is a regular file, refuse unless
#      st_nlink == 1 (this is what rules out a pre-positioned hardlink aliasing an
#      out-of-tree file), refuse on a byte-size mismatch, and refuse on a SHA-256
#      mismatch against the pinned digest.
#   4. Only if all three pass may "semgrep --validate" and the scan run.
gate_exit_semantics: FAIL CLOSED. The gate raises and the pipeline aborts; it never
  degrades to a warning and never proceeds with a partially verified configuration.
  A refusal is a Directive 2 failure and would be recorded as layer_2_status: ERROR.
gate_positive_control: PASS on the live directory - 3 of 3 packs verified, digests as
  listed above, every pack a regular file with st_nlink == 1.

# ADVERSARIAL NEGATIVE-CONTROL MATRIX - five attacks, five distinct refusals.
# Each fixture was created, the gate was run, and the fixture was reverted; the
# positive control was then re-run and reproduced all three digests exactly.
#   NC1 extra YAML injected into the ignored directory
#       -> "rules directory listing is not the allowlist (unexpected=['zz-injected.yaml'])"
#   NC2 an allowlisted pack replaced by a symlink pointing outside the repository
#       -> "symlink refused on path component: .../security-audit/rules/secrets.yaml"
#   NC3 one byte mutated inside an allowlisted pack
#       -> "secrets.yaml: sha256 5b26936... != pinned 139b35ad..."
#   NC4 an allowlisted pack given a second hardlink
#       -> "abnormal link count 2 on .../security-audit/rules/secrets.yaml"
#   NC5 an allowlisted pack removed
#       -> "rules directory listing is not the allowlist (missing=['owasp-top-ten.yaml'])"
gate_negative_controls_run: 5
gate_negative_controls_failed_closed: 5 of 5, each with a specific reason (never an
  empty or generic message - an unexplained refusal would itself be a silent failure)
gate_state_after_controls: fully restored; all three digests re-verified; the working
  tree returned to zero modified paths.

# POST-SCAN TAMPER ASSERTION (defence in depth, in case a gate were ever bypassed):
#   the emitted SARIF must still carry EXACTLY 725 driver rules and EVERY driver rule
#   id and every result ruleId must begin "security-audit.rules.". An injected pack
#   changes the loaded rule count, and a rule loaded from anywhere else cannot carry
#   the configuration-derived prefix, so either condition detects contamination after
#   the fact. Both assertions are evaluated on every scan and recorded in the Layer 2
#   section.

# =============================================================================
# CONTROL 2 (F8) - PATH-SAFE ATOMIC ARTIFACT WRITER
# =============================================================================
# Contract: every artifact published into security-audit/ is written through a
# verified-root, no-follow, exclusive-temporary, fsynced, atomically renamed path.
# No artifact is ever written by plain shell redirection into the ignored tree again.
#
# PROTOCOL (in order; any failure aborts the write and leaves the previous artifact
# untouched):
#   1. Pin and resolve the repository root once. Refuse an absolute target path,
#      refuse any ".." component, and refuse a target whose lexical join escapes the
#      pinned root - containment is checked BEFORE any filesystem access.
#   2. lstat every path component from the root down: refuse a symlink anywhere on the
#      chain, refuse a non-directory intermediate component, and - when the target
#      already exists - refuse a non-regular file or st_nlink != 1.
#   3. Refuse the payload outright if it carries an ANSI escape byte. Redaction and
#      ANSI stripping are CAPTURE-STAGE transforms (IR4), so the writer is the last
#      place they can be enforced rather than a later cleanup pass.
#   4. Open a same-directory temporary with O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_WRONLY|O_NOFOLLOW
#      (plus O_CLOEXEC). O_EXCL is what makes a squatted temporary name a refusal
#      instead of a clobber; O_NOFOLLOW is what makes a symlinked temporary name a
#      refusal instead of a redirect.
#   5. Write, flush, fsync the file descriptor, and assert the written size.
#   6. Read the temporary back and compare its SHA-256 to the intended payload.
#   7. chmod to 0644, then os.rename onto the target - a single atomic operation, so a
#      reader never observes a partial artifact and no window exists between validate
#      and publish (this is the CWE-367 half of the finding).
#   8. fsync the containing DIRECTORY so the rename itself is durable.
#   9. Re-verify the published inode: regular file, st_nlink == 1, digest as intended.
#  10. On any exception, unlink the temporary so no partial file is left behind for a
#      later run to trip over.
writer_positive_controls: 2 of 2 PASS - fresh create (9000 bytes) and atomic replace of
  an existing regular file; zero leftover temporaries in either case.

# ADVERSARIAL NEGATIVE-CONTROL MATRIX - seven attacks, seven refusals:
#   NC1 destination pre-positioned as a symlink to a file OUTSIDE the repository
#       -> refused; the out-of-tree victim file was verified byte-unchanged afterwards
#   NC2 a symlinked DIRECTORY component on the destination path -> refused
#   NC3 destination inode carrying a second hardlink -> refused (abnormal link count)
#   NC4 the same-directory temporary name squatted -> refused by O_EXCL, and the
#       squatted file was verified NOT clobbered
#   NC5 upward traversal ("security-audit/../escape.txt") -> refused
#   NC6 absolute destination path -> refused
#   NC7 payload carrying ANSI escape bytes -> refused at capture
writer_negative_controls_run: 7
writer_negative_controls_refused: 7 of 7
writer_functional_after_refusals: YES - the positive control was re-run after all seven
  refusals and reproduced the intended digest, so the control is fail-closed without
  being fail-stuck.
writer_scope: every artifact republished by the remediation pass - results-semgrep.sarif,
  results-semgrep.json, sink-inventory.txt, results-osv.json, codebase-profile.txt,
  layer-status.json and this file - is published through this writer.
writer_scratch_fixtures_location: ephemeral, OUTSIDE the repository; the two in-tree
  fixture paths used during the negative controls were removed and the working tree was
  re-verified at zero modified paths.

# =============================================================================
# LAYER 4 (Directive 7) - THE NORMALIZED ARTIFACT, AND EVERY DECISION IT ENCODES
# =============================================================================
# Recorded by the Layer 4 NORMALIZATION pass, which produced
# security-audit/findings-layer-4-osv.json from the raw document above. Appended
# as a new section per this file's additive convention; no existing line is
# altered. The raw artifact remains UNMODIFIED (practice E4), so every
# transformation named here lives in the normalized artifact alone.
# This section exists because the eleven OBLIGATIONS stated earlier say what the
# normalizer MUST do; what follows is what it DID, with the measurement for each.

# ---- OBLIGATION 2 discharged: severity, and the ONE coercion, declared ---------
normalized_severity_source_group_max: 256
normalized_severity_source_database_label: 2
normalized_severity_source_impact_class: 1
# THE SEVERITY-SOURCE CHAIN HAS FOUR STEPS, NOT THREE, and the fourth is named here so
# the documented chain matches the emitted tokens exactly (QA remediation, SECTION 23.5):
#   1 group_max        groups[*].max_severity through the published CVSS bands - 256
#   2 database_label   the advisory's own severity label when no score exists - 2
#   3 impact_class     the advisory CLASS, used only for OSV MAL- (malicious package)
#                      records, which carry no CVSS vector, no group maximum and no
#                      database label at all - 1, namely http|MAL-2025-22760. It is a
#                      DERIVATION, not a floor: a confirmed-malicious package that
#                      resolves in the dependency graph executes attacker-authored code
#                      at install and load time, which is a high-impact outcome, and the
#                      likelihood of that path being taken is expressed separately in the
#                      reachability field rather than folded into severity. The record
#                      carries a 400+ character severity_basis stating exactly this.
#   4 unknown          the terminal fallback, RETAINED IN THE CHAIN but NOT EXERCISED on
#                      this run - 0 records. AAP verification check 9 closes the emitted
#                      vocabulary at critical|high|medium|low, so a record that reached
#                      this step would have to be emitted at the vocabulary FLOOR with
#                      the coercion declared on the record; no record does.
# The entry that stood here before this pass read "normalized_severity_source_unknown: 1"
# and is superseded: it was written before the impact_class derivation existed, it does
# not match the token the emitted record carries, and it is the reason a reviewer
# applying the three-step chain to the raw data could not reproduce the artifact. The
# ADVISORY-LEVEL census earlier in this file still says unknown, correctly, because at
# that stage nothing had been derived yet.
normalized_severity_source_impact_class_corroboration: FIRST-PARTY AND EXTERNAL, both
  re-measured during the QA remediation pass rather than assumed.
  FIRST PARTY, from the preserved raw scan security-audit/results-osv.json: the http
  package block carries package {name http, version 0.0.1-security, ecosystem npm}; its
  MAL-2025-22760 vulnerability object has NO severity key at all; its affected[0] carries
  only database_specific, package and ranges - no versions array; aliases is absent; and
  the enclosing group is {ids [MAL-2025-22760], aliases [MAL-2025-22760], max_severity ""}
  - an EMPTY STRING, not a number. Step 1 of the chain therefore cannot resolve, step 2
  has no label to read, and step 3 is the only step that can produce a value at all.
  EXTERNAL, read from the upstream record itself: the published advisory presents no
  severity row, no CVSS vector and no numeric score in either its HTML view or its
  machine-readable form, lists no alias and no CVE, and its details text reads "The
  package http was found to contain malicious code." Two independent sources therefore
  agree that no score exists to derive from, which is what makes impact_class a
  derivation of last resort rather than a preference.
normalized_version_provenance_mal_record: the version 0.0.1-security carried in this
  record's versions array and named in its description comes from the SCANNER'S LOCKFILE
  MATCH (results-osv.json packages[].package.version, corroborated at yarn.lock lines
  24667-24669 "http@npm:0.0.1-security"), NOT from the advisory. The advisory expresses
  its affected set only as a SEMVER range with a single Introduced 0 event and no Fixed
  event, so 0.0.1-security is INSIDE the unbounded affected range rather than enumerated
  by the advisory. Stated explicitly so no reader attributes the version to OSV.
normalized_cwe_506_abstraction_note: CWE-506 Embedded Malicious Code is a CLASS-level
  entry that its authority marks usable for real-world mapping only with careful review,
  and it has four Base-level children - 507 Trojan Horse, 510 Trapdoor, 511 Logic/Time
  Bomb and 512 Spyware. The "most specific CWE" obligation was therefore re-tested
  against those children during this pass: MAL-2025-22760 supplies NO behavioural detail
  whatsoever (its entire details text is the single sentence quoted above), so no Base
  child can be substantiated from the evidence, and the Class-level entry is the most
  specific classification the advisory actually supports. Recorded because choosing a
  Base child here would be a guess dressed as precision, which practice E8 forbids. The
  official name of the entry was re-read from the authority and matches the value used in
  the artifact character for character: "Embedded Malicious Code".

# THE ONE PLACE THE NORMALIZED CENSUS DIFFERS FROM THE RAW CENSUS ABOVE, stated

#                     token is not emittable at all.

# band, and the coercion is declared THREE times on the record itself so that it is
# reported and never quietly rounded:

#                    severity floored, CWE-506 inferred from the malicious-code
#                    evidence" - so the same sentence that discloses the severity
#                    coercion also discloses that the CWE is an inference, not an
#                    OSV-supplied value (see OBLIGATION 3 and 5 discharges below)
# Flooring rather than inflating is the honest direction: raising a scoreless record
# to critical would INVENT a score, which practice E8 forbids more strongly than the
# coercion the closed vocabulary compels. The finding's real weight is carried by its
# content - a MALICIOUS-PACKAGE advisory against a DIRECT PRODUCTION dependency of
# @calcom/api-proxy, recorded in the artifact's direct_dependency_in field as
# apps/api/package.json - not by a number the database never supplied.
