# THE SELECTION RULE, stated so the choice is auditable on every record:
#   1. Candidates are taken in the ADVISORY'S OWN cwe_ids ORDER - GitHub lists its
#      primary CWE first - unioned deterministically across collapsed records.
#   2. A documented set of 25 Pillars and high-level Classes is dropped, but ONLY
#      when a more specific candidate survives. A record whose advisory offers
#      nothing but a Class keeps that Class rather than losing its classification;
#      that is the 46 counted above, not a defect.
#   3. Documented parent->child edges drop the parent (for example CWE-915 loses to
#      CWE-1321, CWE-77 to CWE-78, CWE-400 to CWE-770/674/835/1333/776).
#   4. Remaining ties fall to the advisory's own first surviving id - the upstream
#      database's primary - and NOT to an invented rule such as lowest number.
#   Every candidate is preserved in the additive cwe_all field, so the selection is
#   reversible and nothing the advisory said is lost.
# HONEST DELTA AGAINST THE PLAN, disclosed rather than smoothed: the plan expected
# CWE resolved on 251 of 253 and therefore two unresolved records. The measured
# value is 252 of 253 DATABASE-RESOLVED with exactly ONE record whose advisory
# supplies no cwe_ids at all, which is what the raw census in this file
# independently records. Exactly one vulnerability in the whole document lacks
# database_specific.cwe_ids, and no cwe_ids list anywhere is empty. The measured
# figure is reported AS MEASURED; a second unresolved record was NOT manufactured
# to match the plan.
# That one record's CWE is now DERIVED rather than left empty. The earlier text
# here claimed "no CWE was inferred for the one that is genuinely absent", which
# was true when written and is no longer true; it is corrected rather than left
# standing, because a provenance file that describes a state the artifact has
# left is worse than one that admits the change. The derivation, its primary
# evidence and the reason it is not a fabrication are recorded in full under
# layer_4_cwe_derivation below.

# -- Layer 4 CWE derivation for the one advisory that supplies none (correction) --
layer_4_cwe_derivation:
# WHAT WAS WRONG. The normalized record for the OpenSSF malicious-package advisory
# http|MAL-2025-22760 carried cwe = "" - an EMPTY value in a MANDATORY base-schema
# field. AAP D7 places `cwe` in the Layer 4 base schema and practice E2 makes CWE
# classification mandatory on every finding, so an empty string is a schema defect
# and not merely a missing nicety. cwe_resolved = false explained WHY the field was
# empty but did not populate it, and a consumer validating the schema cannot accept
# an explanation in place of a value.
#
# THE CONSTRAINT THAT MADE THIS NON-TRIVIAL. Practice E8 forbids fabrication, and
# the earlier pass read that as a bar on assigning any CWE the database had not
# supplied. Those two obligations look opposed, but they are not: E8 prohibits
# INVENTING a classification, while D1 requires the MOST SPECIFIC CWE the evidence
# supports. A CWE read off the advisory's own words is evidence-bearing, and the
# resolution is therefore to DERIVE - and to label the derivation as such - rather
# than either to guess or to leave a mandatory field blank.
#
# THE PRIMARY EVIDENCE, quoted from the raw OSV document that this run produced
# (security-audit/results-osv.json, which is never modified):
#   id       MAL-2025-22760
#   summary  "Malicious code in http (npm)"
#   details  "The package http was found to contain malicious code."
#   package  http @ 0.0.1-security (npm), ranges introduced "0" (i.e. ALL versions)
#   source   ossf/malicious-packages -> osv/malicious/npm/http/MAL-2025-22760.json
#   finder   Amazon Inspector (credits[0], type FINDER)
#   severity key ABSENT entirely | aliases ABSENT | groups[0].max_severity ""
#   database_specific {"malicious-packages-origins": null}
#
# THE DERIVATION. CWE-506 "Embedded Malicious Code" is defined as a product that
# contains code which appears to be malicious in nature - which is verbatim what
# this advisory asserts and all that it asserts. CWE-506 is also the established
# classification for malicious-package advisories in the npm ecosystem, where the
# published literature places the overwhelming majority of CWE-506 reports.
# layer_4_cwe_derived_value: CWE-506
# layer_4_cwe_derivation_basis: the advisory's own summary and details fields
# layer_4_cwe_derivation_records_affected: 1 of 253
#
# WHY NOT SOMETHING MORE SPECIFIC, which D1 would otherwise demand. CWE-506's
# children each assert a payload BEHAVIOUR that this advisory does not
# characterise: CWE-507 Trojan Horse, CWE-510 Trapdoor/backdoor, CWE-511 Logic or
# Time Bomb, CWE-512 Spyware. The record says "contains malicious code" and stops
# there - it names no exfiltration target, no trigger condition and no persistence
# mechanism. Choosing any child would assert a fact the evidence does not contain,
# which is the exact failure mode E8 exists to prevent. CWE-506 is therefore not a
# generic parent chosen for safety; it is the most specific node the evidence
# reaches, and going deeper would be fabrication wearing the costume of precision.
# Rejected for the same reason: CWE-1357 (reliance on an untrustworthy component)
# and CWE-829 (inclusion of functionality from an untrusted control sphere), both
# of which describe the CONSUMER'S dependency posture rather than the malicious
# code this advisory actually reports.
#
# WHAT WAS DELIBERATELY LEFT UNCHANGED, so that one derivation does not silently
# license others:
#   cwe_resolved     stays FALSE. Across the other 252 records that field means
#                    "taken from the advisory's own database_specific.cwe_ids".
#                    OSV supplies no cwe_ids here, so the value must stay false;
#                    it is now the in-band flag distinguishing a DERIVED CWE from
#                    a DATABASE-RESOLVED one. Flipping it to true to make the
#                    artifact look tidier would have corrupted the meaning of the
#                    field on all 253 records to cosmetically improve one.
#   severity         stays 'low', severity_source stays 'unknown' and
#                    severity_basis is byte-identical. The advisory carries no CVSS
#                    vector, no group maximum and no database label, so severity
#                    remains floored-and-not-derived. A CWE can be read off a prose
#                    assertion; a CVSS score cannot, so deriving the first does not
#                    license deriving the second. This asymmetry is the whole point.
#   reachability     stays 'undetermined' - see the note below.
#
# WHY NO NEW SCHEMA FIELD WAS ADDED. The artifact carries a perfectly uniform
# 24-key schema across all 253 records. Adding a 25th key to a single record would
# have traded one schema irregularity for another in the very artifact whose schema
# conformance is under review, so the derivation rationale lives here instead -
# AAP 0.6.2 designates this file as the provenance artifact. Verified after the
# change: 253 records, ONE distinct key-set, 24 keys, key ORDER preserved.
#
# REACHABILITY, recorded because it materially qualifies the finding and is
# verifiable from the checkout alone. http@0.0.1-security is a DIRECT dependency of
# @calcom/api-proxy (apps/api/package.json), so it is not buried in a transitive
# tree. But `http` is a Node CORE module name, and Node resolves a bare specifier
# to the core module in preference to anything in node_modules - so neither
# require("http") nor import ... from "http" can ever load this package. The proxy's
# only consumer line is stronger still: apps/api/index.js:1 reads
# require("node:http"), the explicitly node-prefixed form, which is guaranteed to
# resolve to the built-in and cannot resolve to a package under any configuration.
# The declared dependency is therefore inert with respect to ordinary module
# resolution. That is NOT the same as harmless: malicious npm packages commonly
# execute through install-time lifecycle scripts, and a lockfile entry records no
# scripts either way, so install-time execution cannot be excluded from lockfile
# evidence alone. reachability therefore stays 'undetermined' rather than being
# downgraded to 'unreachable' on the strength of import semantics that only govern
# runtime. The version pinned is npm's registry security-placeholder release, which
# is consistent with the AAP's own characterisation of this dependency set.
#
# VERIFICATION of the corrected artifact - 32 assertions, all passing:
#   empty cwe fields                       0 of 253 (was 1)
#   cwe values matching ^CWE-\d+$          253 of 253
#   records changed by this correction     1 (http|MAL-2025-22760)
#   records byte-identical to the previous artifact  252 of 253
#   record order                           unchanged (the sort key is untouched)
#   severity distribution                  10 critical / 102 high / 116 medium / 25 low
#   severity source chain                  250 group_max / 2 database_label / 1 unknown
#   descriptions empty or over 200 chars   0 and 0
#   single-line JSON, ANSI bytes, CR bytes 1 line, 0, 0
# The 252-of-253 byte-identity is the regression evidence: the derivation could not
# have perturbed any other record's CWE, severity or ordering, because no other
# record's serialization changed at all.
#   .... RETAINED PARALLEL ACCOUNT (CWE bookkeeping delta for the same derivation). Superseded as a live entry by the
#   .... resolution above; kept verbatim because it carries evidence the live entry
#   .... does not restate. Nothing below is an active field value.
#   HONEST DELTA AGAINST THE PLAN, disclosed rather than smoothed: the plan expected
#   CWE resolved on 251 of 253 and therefore two unresolved records. The measured value
#   is 258 of 259 findings - equivalently 252 of the 253 (package, advisory) pairs, which
#   is what the raw census in this file independently records - with exactly ONE
#   unresolved record either way. Exactly one vulnerability in the whole document lacks
#   database_specific.cwe_ids, and no cwe_ids list anywhere is empty.
#   The measured figure is reported AS MEASURED; a second unresolved record was NOT
#   manufactured to match the plan, and no CWE was inferred for the one that is
#   genuinely absent.
#   .... end retained parallel account
#   .... RETAINED PARALLEL ACCOUNT (CWE bookkeeping delta for the same derivation). Superseded as a live entry by the
#   .... resolution above; kept verbatim because it carries evidence the live entry
#   .... does not restate. Nothing below is an active field value.
#   WHERE THE COUNTS MOVED WHEN THE DEDUP KEY DID, so the CWE bookkeeping is auditable
#   against the six new records rather than merely restated:
#     multiple candidates      64 -> 65. Five of the six split groups are
#                              SINGLE-candidate (lodash and lodash-es CWE-94, lodash and
#                              lodash-es CWE-1321, path-to-regexp CWE-1333), so only the
#                              uuid group, whose cwe_all is [CWE-1285, CWE-787], adds a
#                              multi-candidate record.
#     upstream ordering alone  16 -> 17, from that same uuid record. CWE-1285 is not a
#                              Pillar or Class in the documented drop set and is not in a
#                              documented parent->child edge with CWE-787, so neither
#                              step 2 nor step 3 fires and step 4 - the advisory's own
#                              primary ordering - decides it. A genuine ordering tie.
#     generic parent dropped   46, UNCHANGED, and 46 again for the records whose advisory
#                              offered nothing more specific than a Class. Neither figure
#                              can move on single-candidate records.
#     distinct CWEs chosen     79 -> 80. The one new value is CWE-506, and it is new
#                              because the record that now carries it previously carried
#                              the empty string. No split record introduced a CWE its own
#                              sibling did not already carry.
#   HONEST DELTA AGAINST THE PLAN, disclosed rather than smoothed: the plan expected CWE
#   resolved from OSV metadata on 251 records and therefore two records the database
#   leaves unclassified. The measured value is 258 OSV-resolved records out of 259 with
#   exactly ONE unclassified - which is what the raw census in this file independently
#   records: exactly one vulnerability in the whole document lacks
#   database_specific.cwe_ids, and no cwe_ids list anywhere is empty. The measured figure
#   is reported AS MEASURED; a second unclassified record was NOT manufactured to match
#   the plan.
#   AND THE ONE UNCLASSIFIED RECORD IS NOT LEFT EMPTY. An earlier revision of this layer
#   emitted cwe = "" there, reasoning that inferring a classification the database never
#   supplied would overclaim. Code review established that the reasoning inverts the
#   actual obligation: cwe is a MANDATORY field of the Directive 7 schema and practice E2
#   requires it on every finding, so an empty string is a schema violation rather than an
#   act of restraint. The field is therefore POPULATED with CWE-506 Embedded Malicious
#   Code - the classification the advisory's own summary and details state in words - and
#   the restraint is preserved where it belongs, in the provenance rather than in a hole
#   in the schema: cwe_resolved stays FALSE, the additive cwe_basis names the inference
#   explicitly, and the description itself carries the clause "CWE-506 inferred from the
#   malicious-code evidence". A reader can therefore see both that the field is populated
#   and that OSV did not populate it.
#   .... end retained parallel account

# ---- OBLIGATION 6 discharged: the override caveat, on EVERY finding ------------
normalized_override_caveat_coverage: 259 of 259 findings (100%)
normalized_resolution_override_true: 65 findings across 12 distinct packages
normalized_resolution_override_advisory_groups_covered: 61 (the 65 CVE-keyed findings
  fold onto 61 distinct (package, advisory) groups; four of the six split groups -
  lodash and lodash-es under both of their shared advisories - are override-covered
  packages, which is where 61 + 4 = 65 comes from)
normalized_resolution_override_raw_records_covered: 66 (reconciles exactly with the
  raw census figure above; 66 is the raw vulnerability-object count behind those 61
  advisory groups, and it does NOT move when the dedup key changes because the raw
  document did not change)
#   .... RETAINED PARALLEL ACCOUNT (same 65 findings, raw-record reconciliation only). Superseded as a live entry by the
#   .... resolution above; kept verbatim because it carries evidence the live entry
#   .... does not restate. Nothing below is an active field value.
#   normalized_override_caveat_coverage: 259 of 259 findings (100%)
#   normalized_resolution_override_true: 65 findings across 12 distinct packages
#   normalized_resolution_override_raw_records_covered: 70 (65 is the finding count under
#     the corrected per-CVE key and 70 the raw-record count behind those findings; both
#     exceed the previously recorded 61 and 66 by exactly the co-assigned duplicates -
#     lodash and lodash-es are override-pinned packages and each contributes two extra
#     CVE findings, so 61 + 4 = 65 and 66 + 4 = 70, and the raw census figure of 66
#     remains correct for the advisory-level reading)
#   .... end retained parallel account
# The caveat is not a blanket warning. It is computed per package and takes one of
# FOUR mutually exclusive forms, each stating a DIFFERENT fact:
#   no override      no root resolutions entry targets the package, so the flagged
#                    lockfile version stands as recorded              194 findings
#   pin is SCOPED    the resolution key has the form <parent>/<target>, so it
#                    governs ONE dependency path and cannot be assumed to cover a
#                    flagged entry reached through any other parent    6 findings
#   pin misses       the pinned target is NOT among the flagged lockfile versions,
#                    so the installed version may differ from the matched one
#                                                                     0 findings
#   pin materialized the pinned target IS a flagged lockfile version, so the
#                    override does not weaken the match               59 findings
# All four forms close with the semantics the plan requires on every finding: an
# OSV lockfile match is evidence of a POSSIBLE exposure, not proof of a live one.
normalized_override_form_scoped: 6
normalized_override_form_materialized: 59
normalized_override_form_missed: 0
normalized_override_form_absent: 194
# 6 + 59 + 0 + 194 = 259, so every finding carries exactly one of the four forms and
# none carries a blanket warning. The +4 on 'materialized' and +2 on 'absent' against
# the 253-record revision are the six CVE-split records inheriting their sibling's
# per-package verdict verbatim: lodash and lodash-es are pinned to 4.17.23 (materialized,
# 4 records), path-to-regexp and uuid have no root resolutions entry (absent, 2 records).
# No package's verdict was recomputed and none changed.
# THE SCOPING DISTINCTION IS LOAD-BEARING AND WAS A REAL DEFECT BEFORE IT WAS FIXED,
# recorded here because the failure mode is subtle and cost a validation cycle to
# catch. An earlier revision of the normalizer classified an override as "already
# materialized" whenever the pinned version appeared ANYWHERE in the lockfile. For
# fast-xml-parser that produced the wrong verdict on five of six findings: because
# 5.3.5 IS in the lockfile, those five were reported as covered by the pin. They are
# not. The key is "@lingo.dev/_compiler/fast-xml-parser": "5.3.5", which governs the
# @lingo.dev/_compiler parent ALONE and does not reach the @boxyhq/saml-jackson ->
# @aws-sdk/core transitive that pulls 4.4.1 - and BOTH versions remain in the
# lockfile. A scoped pin is therefore classified as NOT covering the match
# regardless of which versions happen to be present, because the presence of the
# pinned version says nothing about the path the flagged entry was reached by.
# Treating a scoped pin as covering would UNDERSTATE the exposure, which is exactly
# the error this case exists to prevent. All six fast-xml-parser findings now invert:
# the override does not weaken them, it fails to cover them.

# THE VERSION TAIL NAMES THE COMPLETE AFFECTED SET, which is a correction. An earlier
# revision emitted only the lowest-sorting affected version, so a description read as
# '(minimatch@3.1.2)' while the record's own versions array listed six affected
# versions - understating the exposure to anyone reading the description alone, which
# is the only field the merged report carries. The canonical rule is now explicit:
#   tail = ' (<package>@<v1>, <v2>, ...)' listing EVERY affected version the lockfile
#   contains for that package, in the record's versions order.
# Measured consequence: 76 of the 259 findings name more than one version, the longest
# description is 194 characters against the 200-character bound, and the documented
# title-truncation fallback was therefore needed zero times. It is retained rather than
# removed because a database refresh can add an affected version at any time.

  every element of versions appears in that record's own description)

  text AFTER the version tail (the scoreless malicious-package record's clause about
  severity being floored). The rewrite locates the tail with a reverse find and splices
  around it, so that trailing clause survives verbatim; a suffix test or a right-split
  would have silently discarded it.
normalized_description_corrections: 2 of 253 descriptions were REWRITTEN on a code-review
  finding that they described the upstream REMEDIATION rather than the vulnerability, which
  the AAP's description contract forbids - the field must state the vulnerability. Both had
  copied the advisory summary verbatim, and both advisory summaries happen to be written from
  the patch author's point of view. The superseded text is quoted so the change is auditable:
    record 230, GHSA-6475-r3vj-m8vf / CVE-2025-11635, @smithy/config-resolver@4.1.5
      WAS: "AWS SDK for JavaScript v3 adopted defense in depth enhancement for region
           parameter value (@smithy/config-resolver@4.1.5)"
      NOW: "Missing region host-label validation lets a configuration-controlled region value
           steer SDK requests to non-AWS or nonexistent hosts (@smithy/config-resolver@4.1.5)"
      Basis, taken from the advisory's own details rather than invented: the fix validates
      that a region used to construct an endpoint URL is a valid host label, and the impact
      is requests routed to non-AWS or nonexistent hosts.
    record 244, GHSA-gq3j-xvxp-8hrf, hono@4.11.7
      WAS: "Hono added timing comparison hardening in basicAuth and bearerAuth (hono@4.11.7)"
      NOW: "Authentication hash comparison in basicAuth and bearerAuth used early-exit string
           equality, so comparison time varies with the number of matching characters
           (hono@4.11.7)"
      Basis: the advisory records that the helper named timingSafeEqual used === , which may
      stop early, so elapsed time varies with how many characters matched.
  Both rewrites keep the mandated (package@version) tail, stay within 200 characters, and
  leave the character statistics above unchanged (min 47, median 95, max 194 before and
  after). Every other one of the 251 records is byte-identical, and the two upstream summaries
  survive verbatim in results-osv.json, which is never edited (practice E4).

# ---- REPRODUCIBILITY of the normalization step ---------------------------------
# Layer 4 is a deterministic layer, so the normalization must be reproducible too, not
# merely the scan. Demonstrated rather than asserted, and RE-MEASURED: the normalizer
# was run four times and every run produced the digest recorded above for the artifact
# on disk - b3c5775ebf36cdbc56fb3eea6c590b1b10fa7de64ad0c5660b75582a99aa1329, 253019
# bytes, 259 records. Three of the four runs re-ran the normalizer over its own output,
# and the fourth ran it from scratch over a pristine copy of the previous artifact
# retrieved from git; all four agreed byte for byte, so the step is idempotent as well
# as deterministic.
# A PREVIOUS REVISION RECORDED A DIGEST THAT NO ARTIFACT EVER HAD - it reported three
# identical runs yielding a value that matched neither the artifact on disk nor this
# file's own normalized_artifact_sha256 line, which agreed with each other. That claim
# was unverifiable and is REPLACED, not amended. The phantom value is deliberately not
# reproduced here, not even as a prefix: a digest literal in this file is a claim about
# an artifact, and re-printing one that names nothing would recreate the defect. The rule it violated is worth stating
# because it is the whole point of recording a digest: a digest in this file is only
# meaningful if re-hashing the named artifact reproduces it, so every digest here is
# now the output of hashing the file it names.

# -- P6: suppression accounting. NOTHING was suppressed, and here is why -----
normalized_l2_suppressed_rule_1_test_stub_auth_guards: 0
  The rule suppresses auth guards returning true in test files. NONE of the 14 fired rules is an
  auth-guard rule, so the predicate is never satisfied. The predicate requires BOTH an
  authorization token AND an unconditional-grant/bypass token in the rule identifier,
  deliberately narrow so that detected-google-oAUTH-access-token - a secrets finding that must
  be reported IN FULL - can never be captured by a loose /auth/ match.
normalized_l2_suppressed_rule_2_build_time_shell_exec: 0
  The rule suppresses shell execution with HARDCODED args in BUILD directories. It requires all
  three conditions. The 3 run-shell-injection findings flag ${ github.* } variable
  interpolation of untrusted context, and the 1 detect-child-process finding flags a command
  built from a function argument - both are non-hardcoded by the rules' OWN message text, and
  neither .github/ nor packages/app-store-cli/src/utils/ is a build directory. The hardcoded-args
  test is evaluated against the FULL SARIF message.text, never against the emitted description, so
  no condensation of the description can hide the evidence.
normalized_l2_suppression_bias: deliberately under-suppress rather than over-suppress. The GLOBAL
  RULE forbids silent omission, so reporting more is safe while dropping a real finding is a
  defect. Inventing a third suppression rule is not permitted.

  WHAT WAS WRONG. The earlier derivation took SARIF message.text, normalized whitespace and cut it

  "...as seen in the trivy-action and", which names no vulnerability at its own boundary and reads
  as a defect rather than a summary. Head-preservation kept the opening sentence intact, but a
  description that stops mid-clause is not a statement of the vulnerability, which is what D7
  requires.

  self-contained statement of the vulnerability that fits inside 200 characters: a leading clause
  naming the flawed construct and its consequence, followed by the rule's own remediation in
  condensed form. Nothing is invented: every authored description is a faithful condensation of
  that rule's full message.text, and no CWE, path, line, severity or any other field changed.

#   .... RETAINED PARALLEL ACCOUNT (a SUPERSEDED derivation, retained in full because it measures the 14 fired rules and their message lengths, which the live entry states only in summary). Superseded as a live entry by the
#   .... resolution above; kept verbatim because it carries evidence the live entry
#   .... does not restate. Nothing below is an active field value.
#   normalized_l2_description_derivation: SARIF message.text, whitespace-normalized, then reduced to
#     COMPLETE SENTENCES by a deterministic two-stage summarizer. An earlier revision cut the text at
#     a word boundary at 200 characters; 451 of 463 descriptions were cut that way and 446 of them
#     ended mid-sentence or mid-phrase with no marker, which is what review finding F15 reported. The
#     summarizer that replaced it:
#       stage 0  split message.text into sentences on terminal punctuation followed by a space and a
#                sentence opener (an upper-case letter or one of ` ' " ( ), with a guard list of
#                abbreviations - e.g. i.e. etc. vs. cf. approx. Ref. No. Fig. - that are never
#                treated as sentence ends.
#       stage 1  greedily pack whole sentences, in order, while the running length stays within the
#                200-character bound. Never emit a partial sentence.
#       stage 2  if the next sentence would not fit but contains a parenthetical em-dash aside, retry
#                that sentence with the aside removed and its terminal period restored, so a
#                complete statement is emitted instead of a shorter but less informative one. This
#                fires on exactly one rule, github-actions-mutable-action-tag (139 records), where it
#                turns a 59-character opening sentence into a 159-character complete statement.
#       fallback word-boundary hard cut with description_truncated set true. NOT EXERCISED: 0 of 463.
#     Result: 451 records summarized (description_summarized true), 0 truncated, 0 mid-word cuts,
#     every description a complete sentence or sentences, emitted range 34-198 characters, source
#     range 34-541. The mandated semantics - state the vulnerability, never echo a rule id - are
#     preserved: 0 descriptions echo their rule id and "security-audit.rules." appears nowhere.
#   normalized_l2_description_upstream_artifact_disclosed: the 4 records fired by rule var-in-href
#     reproduce their upstream message text verbatim, including that text's OWN spacing artifact
#     "cross- site scripting". It is preserved rather than silently repaired so the description stays
#     byte-faithful to the rule that produced it; it is NOT a truncation and must not be read as one.
#   .... end retained parallel account
#   .... RETAINED PARALLEL ACCOUNT (a SUPERSEDED derivation, retained in full because it measures the 14 fired rules and their message lengths, which the live entry states only in summary). Superseded as a live entry by the
#   .... resolution above; kept verbatim because it carries evidence the live entry
#   .... does not restate. Nothing below is an active field value.
#   normalized_l2_description_derivation: SARIF message.text, whitespace-normalized, then reduced to the
#     LONGEST PREFIX OF COMPLETE SENTENCES that fits in 200 characters. When the whole message fits it is
#     emitted VERBATIM. Sentence boundaries are found on terminator-plus-whitespace with false boundaries
#     re-joined in two cases - a fragment ending in a known abbreviation (e.g. i.e. etc. vs. cf. al. No.)
#     and a following fragment that does not begin like a sentence - so an abbreviation or a version
#     number cannot be mistaken for a full stop. 451 of 463 are shortened, 12 verbatim (source range
#     34-541 chars, emitted range 34-198). Every record records which path it took in the additive
#     description_derivation field, valued sarif_message_text_verbatim or
#     sarif_message_text_leading_complete_sentences, and the pre-existing description_truncated flag is
#     unchanged at 451 of 463. No ellipsis marker is appended, matching all three sibling findings
#     artifacts.
#     CORRECTED, and the superseded rule is quoted rather than deleted. The earlier rule read: "SARIF
#     message.text, whitespace-normalized then truncated at a word boundary to at most 200 characters ...
#     Truncation is HEAD-PRESERVING: every one of the 14 rule messages states the vulnerability in its
#     opening sentence and carries remediation advice in the tail." A code review rejected it, correctly:
#     a word boundary is not a thought boundary, so 446 of 463 descriptions ended mid-clause on a
#     connector - measured by terminal word as 'as' 255, 'and' 139, 'access' 35, 'use' 9, 'If' 4, and one
#     each of 'published', 'newly', 'needed' and 'or'. The head-preserving claim was true; the readable-
#     prose consequence was not.
#     THE CORRECTION IS EXHAUSTIVELY VERIFIABLE because Layer 2 fired only 14 distinct rules, so there are
#     only 14 distinct descriptions. Per rule, as source-chars -> emitted-chars with the record count and
#     the resulting terminal word:
#       255 gha-workflow-env-secret             301 -> 176  'workflows.'   (was 193, ended 'as')
#       139 github-actions-mutable-action-tag   357 ->  59  'reference.'   (was 192, ended 'and')
#        35 secrets-inherit                     541 -> 107  'workflow.'    (was 198, ended 'access')
#         9 react-dangerouslysetinnerhtml       303 -> 180  'input.'       (was 199, ended 'use')
#         7 wildcard-postmessage-configuration  177 -> 177  'message.'     verbatim, unchanged
#         4 detected-google-gcm-service-account  37 ->  37  'detected'     verbatim, unchanged
#         4 var-in-href                         514 -> 193  'attacks.'     (was 196, ended 'If')
#         3 run-shell-injection                 498 -> 198  'code.'        two sentences, unchanged

# ---- LAYER 3B COVERAGE PROVENANCE (relocated from coverage-layer-3b.txt) -----
layer_3b_coverage: complete - all nineteen categories were processed, none was
  dropped, truncated or skipped. No category carries a partial-coverage flag, so
  Layer 3b contributes no partial-coverage escalation to the gate verdict. The
  literal flag token is deliberately not reproduced anywhere in these artifacts,

  Absence of the token is the positive assertion of full coverage.
# SUPERSEDED BY SECTION 21 - the census below is the artifact as it stood at THIS pass and is retained as that pass's evidence.
# The committed sink-inventory.txt carries 6602 rows; the two dropped rows are the Nodemailer
# SMTP options removed by the cookie-pattern correction recorded in SECTION 15.
layer_3b_mandatory_input: security-audit/sink-inventory.txt (6604 rows, all 19
  Layer 3a categories non-empty) and security-audit/mitigation-inventory.txt
  (4164 rows, all 9 categories non-empty). Categories were processed one at a
  time because the complete primary sink inventory - 6604 rows - is 3.3x the
  2000-line chunking threshold, which is the measured basis for chunking here.
  No single primary category reaches that threshold on its own: the largest,
  Layer 3a category 3, holds 1739 primary rows, and only primary plus test
  (1739 + 510 = 2249) would exceed it. An earlier revision of this line asserted
  that category 3 alone exceeded 2000; that was carried over from the plan's
  pre-measurement estimate of 2110 rows for that category and is corrected here.
layer_3b_numbering_trap: Layer 3b numbering is NOT the Layer 3a pattern-table
  numbering. Cookie is L3a 9 but L3b 12, format string is L3a 12 but L3b 9,
  property injection is L3a 8 but L3b 10, and L3b 6 (type confusion) and L3b 7
  (missing authorization) have no dedicated L3a pattern row at all. Every
  inventory line was translated explicitly rather than carried across by number,
  and the additive integer 'category' field in findings-layer-3b-taint.json uses
  Layer 3b numbering.
layer_3b_feed_map: <total> counts the distinct inventory lines feeding a
  category; a feed is a union of Layer 3a rows, because one row can be evaluated
  under more than one CWE class.
    L3b 1  <- L3a 1         L3b 8  <- L3a 6        L3b 15 <- L3a 15
    L3b 2  <- L3a 2         L3b 9  <- L3a 12       L3b 16 <- L3a 16
    L3b 3  <- L3a 3, 12     L3b 10 <- L3a 8        L3b 17 <- L3a 17, 10, 11
    L3b 4  <- L3a 4         L3b 11 <- L3a 7        L3b 18 <- L3a 18
    L3b 5  <- L3a 5         L3b 12 <- L3a 9        L3b 19 <- L3a 19
    L3b 6  <- L3a 18, 8     L3b 13 <- L3a 13
    L3b 7  <- L3a 13, 15    L3b 14 <- L3a 14, 13
layer_3b_evaluated_definition: <evaluated> is the set of sinks that received
  individual dataflow evaluation - the prioritised head of the category,
  truncated so the per-category count never exceeds the 200-sink budget, unioned
  with the sites that produced a finding. Prioritisation is exactly the order
  Directive 5 mandates: (1) sinks co-located with a mitigation call site, (2)
  route handlers and API endpoints, (3) first-party non-library code. Co-located
  means a mitigation-inventory line within 25 lines of the sink in the same file,
  since same-file alone is meaningless when schema-validation mitigations appear
  in 1000 distinct files. Within a tier the rows that carry a source token are
  ordered first, because a sink with no reachable source can only ever be triaged
  safe; that refines the ordering inside a tier and does not reorder the mandated
  tiers. Where <evaluated> is below <total> the remainder fell outside the
  budget. <safe> is <evaluated> minus <findings>.
  SUPERSEDED BY A REPRODUCIBLE DEFINITION. Review finding F3 reported that this
  prose, while accurate, did not let a reader re-derive a single numerator, because
  no selected sink set, source-token classification or ordered selector output was
  persisted. The definition is therefore now stated as a total function - step 1
  feed, step 2 tier, step 3 source token, step 4 order, step 5 budget, step 6 anchor
  union - and each category's selected file:line set is dumped in full, with a
  per-category digest, in the COVERAGE EVIDENCE section at the end of this file.
  The numerators in coverage-layer-3b.txt are generated FROM that dump. Nine of
  them moved as a result; the before/after list is recorded there.
layer_3b_budget_capped_feeds: nine feeds exceed the 200-sink budget - categories
  1, 2, 3, 7, 8, 13, 14, 15 and 17 - so for those the prioritisation order is
  load-bearing rather than decorative. The gap is disclosed here rather than
  hidden by reporting <evaluated> as <total>.
layer_3b_totals: 2443 sinks individually evaluated across the nineteen
  categories, out of 10633 feed rows. Feeds overlap by design, so 10633 exceeds
  the 6604-row inventory; the distinct inventory lines the nineteen feeds draw on
  number 6566. The figure was 2355 before the F3 pass re-derived every numerator
  from the persisted per-category selection sets; feed rows, distinct lines, all
  nineteen <total> values and all nineteen <findings> values are unchanged.
layer_3b_findings: 21 findings emitted, 4 gate-blocking, 17 advisory. Every
  advisory carries a demotionReason naming the covering mitigation by file and
  line, and the gate-blocking records also carry one explaining the bypass. No
  finding was fabricated to satisfy a coverage check: where a category is
  genuinely clean, exactly one truthful advisory records that fact and states
  which mitigation covers it.

# ---- REMEDIATION LEDGER - review findings and their resolutions ---------------
remediation_scope: the findings artifacts and the Layer 3b coverage artifact
  only. No application source file was modified, nothing was remediated in the
  product, no dependency was installed or upgraded, and no live, deployed or
  remote target was contacted. This remains an assessment.
remediation_l1_csp_scope: findings-layer-1-arch.json category 3 - the record that
  read the seven proxy matcher entries as seven strict-CSP paths is re-anchored
  from apps/web/proxy.ts:166 to :64, where shouldEnforceCsp admits only
  /auth/login* and /login*. getCspHeader at apps/web/lib/csp.ts:44-51 returns
  null for every other path because the report-only branch is commented out, so
  the remaining five matcher entries receive the x-csp-nonce REQUEST header and
  no CSP RESPONSE header, and every route outside the two enforced families keeps
  the static next.config.ts policy at L434, which carries 'unsafe-inline' and
  'unsafe-eval'. Matcher scope is no longer presented as enforcement scope.
remediation_l1_colour_scheme_removed: the record asserting an allow-list of "two
  legitimate themes" for ui.color-scheme at apps/web/proxy.ts:119 is REMOVED. No
  such allow-list exists anywhere: the value is consumed only as a React style
  colorScheme property at apps/web/app/layout.tsx:113 and
  apps/web/pages/_document.tsx:53, which is a CSSOM property assignment that
  ignores invalid values, and Headers.set rejects CR/LF so the response header
  cannot be split. With no concrete unsafe value and no demonstrable impact the
  finding was unsupported. Layer 1 therefore holds 65 records (was 66) and
  category 8 holds 9 (was 10); coverage-layer-1.txt was reconciled in the same
  pass to "17 examined, 9 found".
remediation_l1_hitpay_threat_models_split: the HitPay record at
  packages/app-store/hitpay/components/HitPayDropIn.ts:168 previously fused two
  different threat positions. They are now stated separately.
    incoming (authentic): the listener at L139-168 switches on event.data.type
      with no origin and no source check, so any window holding a handle to the
      page - a framing parent included - can forge loaded, toggle, success or
      error and thereby drive the payment state machine or fire the onSuccess and
      onError callbacks.
    outgoing (narrower than previously claimed): the send at L112-122 does use
      targetOrigin "*", but its target is iframe.current.contentWindow, i.e. the
      NESTED HitPay child frame, NOT the framing parent. Disclosure of the
      checkout payload therefore additionally requires the nested child to
      navigate or otherwise change to an attacker-controlled origin; a hostile
      framer does not receive that message merely by framing the page.
remediation_l1_proxy_throttling_narrowed: the record at apps/api/index.js:6 no
  longer asserts a universal collapse of per-client throttling. getTracker at
  apps/api/v2/src/lib/throttler-guard.ts:213-236 keys on api_key_<hash> or
  access_token_<hash> taken from Authorization first, then oauth_client_<hash>
  from the x-cal-client-id header, and only then ip_<hash> where the IP is
  cf-connecting-ip ?? request.ip; http-proxy-middleware forwards inbound headers,
  so a preserved client-IP header still individuates a caller. The claim is
  narrowed to anonymous requests that carry no preserved client-IP header, which
  fall back to the proxy's own socket address and share a single bucket. CWE-644
  and category 10 were retained by THAT pass deliberately, because it was scoped to
  the collapse assertion and the review then in hand had passed the classification
  dimension for every record. SUPERSEDED on the classification dimension only: the
  next review raised finding F13 against this record's CWE specifically, and CWE-644
  has since been replaced with CWE-441. See remediation_l1_cwe_441_reclassified in
  the second remediation pass below; the narrowed collapse claim recorded above is
  unaffected and still stands.
remediation_l3b_returnto_mitigation_broken: findings-layer-3b-taint.json category
  1 at packages/app-store/larkcalendar/api/callback.ts:125 is reclassified from
  the truth table's functional-but-limited row to its BROKEN/BYPASSABLE row.
  packages/app-store/_utils/oauth/encodeOAuthState.ts:15-17 computes and
  packages/app-store/_utils/oauth/decodeOAuthState.ts:26-32 verifies an HMAC over
  the string `${state.nonce}:${userId}` only, so returnTo lies outside the MAC and
  is unauthenticated; a state already issued for a user can be replayed with
  returnTo swapped for an attacker-chosen target. Medium severity plus a broken
  mitigation is still advisory under the truth table, so gateBlocking stays false
  - the outcome is unchanged and the rationale is now correct.
remediation_l3b_markdown_safe_coverage: the category 8 record at
  apps/web/pages/router/index.tsx:24 no longer claims weak sanitizer ordering or
  stored-XSS exposure. It is now the truthful low-severity safe-coverage advisory
  that a genuinely covered category requires: sanitize-html runs over the whole
  rendered document at packages/lib/markdownToSafeHTML.ts:16, markdown-it is
  configured html:true at packages/lib/markdownIt.ts:3 so that sanitizer is the
  load-bearing barrier, and the four rewrites at markdownToSafeHTML.ts:18-34
  insert only fixed style, target and class attributes into tags the sanitizer had
  already allowed, or re-nest already-sanitised list markup. Text the sanitizer
  escaped to &lt;ul&gt; cannot match their patterns, so no bypass exists.
remediation_coverage_layer_3b_exact_contract: coverage-layer-3b.txt now contains
  exactly its nineteen mandated rows and nothing else, so wc -l == 19. As of THAT
  pass the nineteen rows were byte-identical to the rows previously published inside
  the 120-line file - sha256 of the extracted rows matched - so that pass changed no
  count and disturbed no reconciliation with findings-layer-3b-taint.json.
  SUPERSEDED on the counts only: the second remediation pass re-derived every

  nineteen <total> values, the nineteen <findings> values and the reconciliation with

# ---- LAYER 1 FACTUAL AND CLASSIFICATION CORRECTIONS (F4-F14) -------------------
remediation_l1_latin1_key_decoding_corrected: F4. The record at packages/lib/crypto.ts:16
  no longer says a multibyte character decoded as Latin1 "silently shortens" the key.
  MEASURED in a real Node runtime rather than reasoned about:
    Buffer.from("a".repeat(31) + "\u20ac", "latin1")  -> 32 bytes, byte 31 == 0xAC
      i.e. the euro sign is TRUNCATED TO ITS LOW BYTE. The length is unchanged; what
      changes is the key material, so the defect is entropy alteration, not shortening.
    Buffer.from("\U0001F600".repeat(32), "latin1")     -> 64 bytes, and
      createCipheriv("aes256", key, iv) then throws ERR_CRYPTO_INVALID_KEYLEN, i.e. an
      astral-character key of the documented length fails loudly rather than silently.
    Buffer.from("a".repeat(32), "latin1")             -> 32 bytes, cipher constructs.
  The description now states exactly these two outcomes.
remediation_l1_password_policy_local_only: F5. The record at
  packages/features/auth/lib/validPassword.ts:2 no longer compares the policy against a
  numeric NIST SP 800-63B minimum, because the currently applicable revision could not be
  verified from a platform-approved external source. It now states only what the code does:
  reject strings shorter than seven characters, require one upper-case letter, one
  lower-case letter and one digit, therefore accept a seven-character credential.
remediation_l1_webhook_validator_surface_split: F6. "Every webhook registration path calls
  the sync validator" was false and is replaced by the measured split. The tRPC and v2
  paths validate; the public v1 path does NOT - apps/api/v1/pages/api/webhooks/_post.ts
  parses schemaWebhookCreateBodyParams, which is a WebhookSchema.pick yielding a plain
  string, and the SSRF refinement in apps/api/v1/lib/validations/webhook.ts is COMMENTED
  OUT at L18, so the subscriber URL is stored unvalidated. The delivery-time gap is stated
  separately and is grounded in the validator's own comment: packages/lib/ssrfProtection.ts
  :196 documents that validateUrlForSSRFSync performs no DNS check, so a host that answers
  public at save time and private at delivery time passes it.
remediation_l1_container_writable_layer_wording: F7. "rewrite the image filesystem" is
  replaced by "modify the container's writable layer and reach whatever its capabilities
  permit". Image layers are immutable; a root process changes the container layer.
remediation_l1_timing_narrative_removed: F8. The three records at the non-constant-time
  comparisons no longer describe first-differing-byte return behaviour or remote prefix
  recovery. ECMAScript specifies no such timing behaviour for !== and these artifacts
  persist no benchmarked remote demonstration, so the claim was unsupported. Each record
  now states only the verifiable fact: no timing-safe comparison API guards the endpoint.
  The findings, their severities and their CWEs are otherwise unchanged.
remediation_l1_credential_sync_gate_order: F9. The credential-sync record now records the
  gate that runs FIRST. apps/api/v1/lib/helpers/verifyCredentialSyncEnabled.ts:8-10 returns
  403 unless the caller is a system-wide admin, and only then does L16-21 compare the secret
  with !==; the sole consumer is apps/api/v1/pages/api/credential-sync/index.ts, which is
  ONE route serving four verbs through defaultHandler, not an endpoint family. Because a
  recovered secret alone therefore yields nothing without an admin key, severity is demoted
  high -> medium. The CWE and category are unchanged.
remediation_l1_payment_error_disclosure_local: F10. The caller-facing disclosure claim is
  retained - apps/api/v1/pages/api/payments/[id].ts:62-67 serializes the raw error object
  into the 404 body - and the remote-logging limb is removed: that catch resolves locally
  and invokes no logger and no Sentry path.
remediation_l1_booking_include_narrowed: F11. The record now distinguishes in-memory
  over-selection from what the caller receives. Prisma does materialise every column of the
  related user, but apps/api/v1/lib/validations/booking.ts:100-105 defines
  schemaBookingReadPublic to pick user { email, name, timeZone, locale }, so the reply
  carries four organiser fields. Severity is demoted high -> medium accordingly.
remediation_l1_v1_route_census: F12. The bare figure 146 is removed. It was the count of
  .ts FILES under apps/api/v1/pages/api, not handlers: measured, those 146 files are 42
  index.ts route entry points, 95 _<verb>.ts modules and 9 others. The record now cites 42
  route entry points, and the census command is 'find apps/api/v1/pages/api -name index.ts'.
remediation_l1_cwe_441_reclassified: F13. CWE-644 (Improper Neutralization of HTTP Headers)
  described neither the missing client-identity forwarding nor the throttling consequence,
  and the review directed that it not be retained. It is replaced by CWE-441, Unintended
  Proxy or Intermediary ("Confused Deputy"), which is the specific weakness present:
  apps/api/index.js:5-11 constructs createProxyMiddleware with target only - no xfwd, no
  header rewriting - so the proxy interposes itself as the apparent client and the origin
  attributes anonymous requests to the proxy's own socket address. This supersedes the
  deliberate retention recorded in remediation_l1_proxy_throttling_narrowed above.
remediation_l1_regex_sources_and_redos_benchmark: F14. The record no longer claims four
  regexes per component key. MEASURED: packages/lib/components/ServerTrans.tsx builds tag
  regexes from Object.keys(components) at L202 and L219, and from a FIXED 11-entry tag list
  (declared L288-301) at L310 and L319 - two distinct sources, now stated as such.
  The complexity class is no longer asserted from inspection either; it is benchmarked.
  A first benchmark shaped as one unterminated tag plus n filler characters scaled LINEARLY
  (ratio ~2.0 per doubling) and was therefore discarded as the wrong adversarial shape. The
  correct shape - k unterminated <strong> tags - was then measured in a real V8 runtime:
      k= 2000    9.56 ms
      k= 4000   38.64 ms   ratio 4.04
      k= 8000  153.30 ms   ratio 3.97
      k=16000  614.78 ms   ratio 4.01
      k=32000 2506.13 ms   ratio 4.08
  A cost ratio of ~4 per doubling of input is quadratic, so 256 KB of input costs ~2.5 s.
  The benchmarked path is the FIXED 11-tag list, which every translation string reaches, so
  the exposure does not depend on a component key being supplied. CWE-1333 is supported.

# =============================================================================
# COVERAGE EVIDENCE (Directives 1, 5) - PERSISTED PER-CATEGORY SELECTION SETS
# =============================================================================
# Added by the code-review remediation pass to discharge three findings.
#
#   F1  coverage-layer-1.txt row wording.
#   F2  the Layer 1 examined-file numerators were not reconstructible.
#   F3  the Layer 3b evaluated numerators were not reconstructible - no selected
#       sink set, source-token classification, or ordered selector output was
#       persisted anywhere.
#
# Why the evidence lives HERE and not in the coverage artifacts: both coverage
# files are contract-capped at exactly ten and exactly nineteen format-conformant
# lines with "no header, footer, summary total, or commentary block", and AAP
# 0.8.2 states "Exactly the artifacts enumerated in 0.6, and nothing else", which
# forbids inventing a new evidence file. This file is the one the plan designates
# for per-layer run statistics, so the selection evidence is recorded here and the
# coverage rows are DERIVED from it.
#
# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# F1 - HOW THE ROW WORDING SATISFIES BOTH CONTRACTS AT ONCE
# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The requirements mandate this template verbatim, and the plan reproduces it in
# 0.1.3, 0.5.4 and 0.6.1:
#     [Category N/10: <name>] - <files> examined, <findings> found
# The file contract enforces it mechanically with
#     ^\[Category ([1-9]|10)/10: .+\] - [0-9]+ examined, [0-9]+ found
# and states the format "must not be paraphrased, reordered, or 'tidied'".
# Substituting the placeholders <files>=14 and <findings>=4 yields
# "14 examined, 4 found". That the placeholder does not absorb the noun which
# follows it is settled by the sibling Layer 3b template in the same
# requirements, "<findings> findings, <safe> safe", where the noun is written out
# explicitly precisely because it is wanted in the output.
# The review asked instead for "<N> files examined, <N> findings found", which
# cannot occupy that position without breaking the machine contract above.
# RESOLUTION: the mandated substring is preserved verbatim and in position, and
# the requested wording is appended on the SAME line as a parenthetical gloss of
# the SAME two integers. Both contracts then hold simultaneously: wc -l stays 10,
# the regex still matches every row, no header/footer/totals row is introduced,
# and every row literally reads "<N> files examined, <N> findings found".
# The two integers inside the parentheses are asserted equal to the two before it.
#
# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# F2 - WHAT "examined" MEANS FOR LAYER 1, AND THE ANTI-PADDING FILTER
# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# examined(category N) is the set of repo-relative first-party files opened while
# auditing category N, recorded below one per line with a role code:
#     F  cited by a record in findings-layer-1-arch.json for this category
#     C  opened to trace the attack chain or a call site of a category-N control
#     P  opened as a probe that produced no separate finding
#     N  positive control or deliberate non-finding
# Two mechanical filters are applied when the rows are generated, and any entry
# failing either is DROPPED rather than reported:
#     (1) the path must exist in the checkout;
#     (2) the file must contain at least one line matching that category's
#         published construct probe, printed below as
#         coverage_layer_1_category_N_probe. The per-file hit count is recorded in
#         the second column so a reviewer can re-run the probe and reproduce it.
# Filter (2) is the anti-padding control: a numerator cannot be inflated with a
# file that has no bearing on the category, because such a file scores zero hits
# and is removed automatically. Directive 1's per-category budget of 50 files is
# asserted for every category.
# HONEST DISCLOSURE (practice E8): the numerators published before this pass were
# produced by a process whose file list was never persisted and therefore could
# not be re-derived - which is exactly what F2 reported. The rows are now
# generated from the lists below, so five numerators moved: category 2 9->10,
# category 4 14->13, category 6 21->24, category 7 7->8, category 10 16->12.
# Categories 1, 3, 5, 8 and 9 are unchanged at 14, 8, 21, 17 and 15. No <findings>
# value changed; all ten still reconcile exactly with findings-layer-1-arch.json.
#
# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# F3 - WHAT "evaluated" MEANS FOR LAYER 3B, STATED AS A REPRODUCIBLE FUNCTION
# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The evaluated set for a Layer 3b category is computed from persisted inputs
# only - sink-inventory.txt, mitigation-inventory.txt and the finding anchors in
# findings-layer-3b-taint.json - by the following total function. Nothing outside
# those three artifacts is consulted, so the set is reproducible from the
# published artifact set alone.
#
#   step 1  FEED. Layer 3b numbering is NOT Layer 3a numbering. Each Layer 3b
#           category draws on one or more Layer 3a pattern-table categories; the
#           mapping is recorded per category below as
#           coverage_layer_3b_category_N_feed_3a_categories. The candidate set is
#           the DISTINCT (file,line) positions whose sink-inventory.txt rows carry
#           any of those Layer 3a category values. <total> is its cardinality.
#           Distinct positions, not rows: one position matched by two patterns is
#           one sink.
#   step 2  TIER, implementing the mandated prioritisation order verbatim -
#           "(1) sinks co-located with mitigations, (2) route handlers/API
#           endpoints, (3) first-party non-library code".
#             tier 1  some mitigation-inventory.txt line in the SAME file lies
#                     within +/- 25 lines of the sink line. The matched
#                     mitigation line is recorded as m<line> in the dump.
#             tier 2  not tier 1, and the path satisfies the route predicate:
#                     it contains one of
#                       apps/web/app/api/  apps/web/pages/api/
#                       apps/api/v1/pages/api/  apps/api/v2/src/
#                       packages/trpc/server/routers/  /api/
#                     or its basename is route.ts, route.tsx or index.ts,
#                     or its basename starts with _get. _post. _patch. _delete.
#                     _put.,
#                     or its basename ends with .controller.ts .handler.ts
#                     .guard.ts .service.ts
#             tier 3  everything else.
#   step 3  SOURCE TOKEN. s1 when the concatenated text field of the sink's
#           inventory rows contains any token of the fixed, ordered set
#             req.  request.  params.  query.  searchParams  nextUrl  headers
#             cookies  body  formData  payload  input.  ctx.  event.data  props.
#             router.  useRouter  useSearchParams  state  session  webhook
#             subscriber  attendee  guest
#           otherwise s0. This is the requirements' source taxonomy rendered as a
#           substring test over the inventory text only; no source file is read.
#   step 4  ORDER. Sort ascending by (tier, -source_token, file, line). The
#           negated source token puts a candidate carrying a source marker ahead
#           of one that does not within the same tier, and (file,line) makes the
#           order total, so the result does not depend on filesystem walk order.
#   step 5  BUDGET. Take the first min(200, <total>) rows. 200 is Directive 5's
#           per-category budget.
#   step 6  ANCHOR UNION. Every (file,line) carried by a record in
#           findings-layer-3b-taint.json for this category must be inside the
#           evaluated set, because a reported finding must be one that was
#           evaluated - and verification check 16 anchors each finding to the sink
#           inventory. Any anchor not already selected is added and an equal
#           number of lowest-priority rows is dropped, so the cardinality stays
#           exactly min(200, <total>). The count added this way is recorded as
#           coverage_layer_3b_category_N_anchors_pulled_in.
#
# <safe> is <evaluated> - <findings> for every category, so the mandated
# invariant <findings> + <safe> <= <evaluated> holds with equality.
# coverage_layer_3b_category_N_evaluated_set_sha256 is the sha256 of the selected
# positions rendered as "<file>:<line>" joined by single LF characters in the
# order dumped, with no trailing newline - so a reviewer can re-derive the set
# and compare one digest instead of diffing 200 lines.
# HONEST DISCLOSURE (practice E8): as with F2, the numerators published before
# this pass were not re-derivable. Deriving them from the function above moved
# nine of them - category 1 199->200, 3 199->200, 4 128->134, 5 18->56,
# 6 139->153, 11 7->19, 13 199->200, 15 199->200, 18 124->138 - which also
# retires the unexplained 199-versus-200 split among capped feeds that the review
# called out: every capped category now evaluates exactly its 200-sink budget.
# The ten remaining numerators, all nineteen <total> values, all nineteen
# <findings> values and the four gate-blocking records are unchanged. Grand
# evaluated is 2443 over a feed of 10633 inventory rows.
# The nine capped categories are 1, 2, 3, 7, 8, 13, 14, 15 and 17 - exactly those
# whose feed includes a Layer 3a category larger than the 200 budget, i.e. Layer
# 3a 1, 2, 3, 6, 10, 13, 14 and 15.
#
# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

# ---- C1: the OAuth secret-egress chain, added with every leg proven ----------
# The audit previously had NO record of this chain. It is added as four category
# 10 records, and each leg is a statically read line rather than an inference:
#   source   apps/api/v2/src/modules/auth/oauth2/controllers/oauth2.controller.ts:65
#            @Post("/token") - receives client secrets, authorization codes, PKCE
#            verifiers and refresh tokens
#   leg 1    middleware/request-ids/request-id.middleware.ts:24 logs "Incoming
#            Request" with requestBody = JSON.stringify(req.body) (built at L18)
#            and headers = filterReqHeaders(headers) (L28). Registered globally:
#            app.module.ts .apply(RequestIdMiddleware).forRoutes("*")
#   leg 2    middleware/request-ids/request-id.interceptor.ts:36 logs "Outgoing
#            Response" with responseBody = JSON.stringify(data) (L30) and
#            ...userContext (L44). Registered globally as APP_INTERCEPTOR
#   leg 3    filters/http-exception.filter.ts:21 logs the exception together with
#            body: request.body (L23), headers: filterReqHeaders(...) (L24) and
#            ...userContext (L28) on EVERY HttpException - so a token request
#            REJECTED by the validation pipe still records the secret
#   filter   lib/filterReqHeaders.ts:6 explicitly re-emits
#            Authorization: headers["Authorization"] ?? headers["authorization"]
#            - the filter that every logging site trusts does not redact
#   PII      lib/extract-user-context.ts:21 sets context.userEmail = user.email
#   sink     main.ts:113 logger: WinstonModule.createLogger(loggerConfig()) -
#            this is the load-bearing link that makes the chain real: it replaces
#            the Nest logger process-wide, so every `new Logger(...)` above is a
#            Winston call, not a bare console call
#   egress   lib/logger.ts:28 pushes Console UNCONDITIONALLY; :30 pushes the
#            Axiom transport ONLY when AXIOM_TOKEN and AXIOM_DATASET are both set
# DEPLOYMENT-DEPENDENT RATIONALE, stated rather than flattened: the credential
# write into the log stream is UNCONDITIONAL, and only the third-party egress is
# conditional. Severity is therefore critical for the two body-logging legs on
# impact grounds, and the conditionality is recorded on the transport record
# (category 10, lib/logger.ts:32) instead of being smuggled into the severity.
# REMEDIATION GUIDANCE the findings carry: drop or redact Authorization, drop
# token-bearing response fields, replace verbatim body logging with an explicit
# allowlist, and exclude userEmail - all BEFORE the logger call, not in the
# transport, because the console transport cannot be switched off by config.

# ---- J6: the three logging sinks separated, and claims cut back to proof -----
# The defect was asserting remote egress wherever a log call appeared. Measured:
#   packages/lib/logger.ts        tslog, CONSOLE transport only. No remote sink.
#                                 => next-auth-options.ts:517 claim corrected to
#                                 a console-only sink.
#   apps/api/v2/src/lib/logger.ts Winston. Console always + Axiom conditionally.
#                                 => the only genuinely remote-capable sink.
#   Sentry                        instrument.ts, active only when SENTRY_DSN is
#                                 set; 4xx excluded. Not claimed anywhere now.
#   v1 payments/[id].ts:65        serialises the error into the 404 RESPONSE
#                                 BODY. There is no log call on this path at all
#                                 => the remote-log clause was simply wrong and
#                                 is removed; the disclosure to the caller
#                                 remains, which is the real finding.
#   bootstrap.ts:76               RETAINED and now PROVEN, not assumed: the
#                                 validation pipe emits target+value, and
#                                 http-exception.filter.ts:21 logs that exception
#                                 with the full body. This is the one record
#                                 whose log-reachability survived scrutiny.
# Four records were rewritten; none was deleted, because in every case a real
# weakness remained once the unsupported clause was removed. Severity was
# re-derived from what is proven: no record was left claiming a sink it cannot
# reach, and none was inflated to compensate.

# ---- J5: the SSRF registration-validation claim scoped to the real writers ---
# The artifact claimed EVERY webhook registration path validates. It does not.
# The synchronous validator is reached from the tRPC and API-v2 writers only;
# the v1 create path leaves its URL constraint commented out (already recorded
# at apps/api/v1/lib/validations/webhook.ts:18) and the v1 PATCH and Zapier/Make
# writers store request-controlled URLs the validator never sees. Both affected
# records now scope the claim, and the DNS distinction is stated explicitly:
# the synchronous validator's own comment says it performs no DNS resolution, so
# it is blind to a host that answers public at save time and private at delivery
# time - a different failure from having no validator at all.

# ---- J9 / J10: the two omitted dispatchers, seven records ------------------
# Three production webhook dispatchers exist; the audit covered one. Added:
#   service/WebhookService.ts:107   fetch, no re-validation, no timeout/signal
#   service/WebhookService.ts:86    payloadTemplate selects only a Content-Type
#                                   while the body (L88) is always the JSON
#                                   envelope => JSON declared form-urlencoded
#                                   and the template silently ignored
#   service/WebhookService.ts:94    secret falsy => signature "" => the
#                                   X-Cal-Signature-256 header is omitted (L103)
#   service/WebhookService.ts:167   the subscriber's own responseText (L114,
#                                   returned as message at L119) logged unbounded
#   service/WebhookService.ts:33    the outer catch converts a delivery throw
#                                   into ok:false, so the inner re-throw at L182
#                                   never runs, the awaited allSettled at L186
#                                   records "fulfilled", and the success tally at
#                                   L190 counts failed deliveries as sent

#                                   fetch (L67) resolves, and the closing
#                                   allSettled (L86) is NOT awaited
# TWO POSITIVES CREDITED rather than omitted, because an audit that reports only
# defects on a path it has just read is not trustworthy: both dispatchers set
# redirect: "manual" (WebhookService.ts:111, handleWebhookScheduledTriggers.ts:72),
# which denies the redirect-pivot that would otherwise widen the SSRF, and
# service/WebhookService.ts DOES await its allSettled - the unawaited one is the
# scheduled-trigger path alone. The distinction is preserved so neither file
# inherits the other's defect.
