# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 23.4  ISSUE 4 (MINOR) - the emitted order did not realize the declared sort key
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# REPORTED: findings-layer-4-osv.json violated its own declared normalized_sort_key. The
# array was two sorted runs, 250 records then 9, with a single descent at index 250, and
# its severity blocks were non-contiguous (critical 10, high 106, medium 118, low 16,
# high 1, low 8). The QA tested ten plausible readings of the declared key and the shipped
# file was fully sorted under none of them.
# ROOT CAUSE, confirmed and now on the record next to the key itself: the pass that re-keyed
# the artifact from (package_name, advisory_id) to the directive's literal
# (package_name, CVE_ID) added six records and APPENDED them after the already-sorted body.
# Five of the six landed where the key would have placed them anyway. The sixth,
# http|MAL-2025-22760, did not, because the SAME pass had also moved that record from the
# low band to high through the impact_class derivation - which is why issues 4, 5 and 6 all
# converge on one record.
# RESOLUTION: all 259 records are re-emitted under the declared key. Exactly ONE record
# relocates, index 250 -> 47, between hono|CVE-2026-54290 and i18next-fs-backend|
# CVE-2026-41693. The other 258 keep their relative order exactly - asserted, not assumed -
# and 204 of them shift index by one purely as a consequence of the insertion.
# VERIFIED: zero descents under the four-component reading and under a stricter
# five-component reading that separates cve from advisory; severity blocks now contiguous
# critical 10 / high 107 / medium 118 / low 24, which is the census this file declares;
# the record set is proven unchanged by comparing sorted dedup_key multisets (259 in, 259
# out, 0 duplicates, 0 loss, 0 extras). The array is now sorted under every alternative
# reading re-tested, where before it satisfied none.
# WHY THIS MATTERED DESPITE BOUNDED IMPACT: no mandated field and no verification check
# tests ordering, so nothing was wrong in the findings themselves. But the declared key
# exists so that a baseline diff sees only real movement, and a later milestone persists
# baseline.json. Correcting the order BEFORE a baseline exists costs one record's position;
# correcting it after would have manufactured spurious drift across 204 rows.
#
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 23.5  ISSUE 5 (INFO) - impact_class was a fourth severity source outside the chain
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# REPORTED: applying the documented group_max -> database_label -> unknown chain to the
# live raw data resolves http|MAL-2025-22760 to "unknown", while the artifact emits
# severity_source "impact_class" and severity "high" - a fourth source.
# RESOLUTION: the chain is now declared with FOUR steps and per-step counts (group_max 256,
# database_label 2, impact_class 1, unknown 0 and not exercised), and the two live entries
# that still described the three-step chain were corrected: severity_source_unknown: 1 is
# superseded by severity_source_impact_class: 1, and normalized_severity_low: 25 is
# corrected to 24. The arithmetic tell for the stale figure is recorded alongside it -
# 10 + 107 + 118 + 25 = 260 cannot reconcile against the declared total of 259, while
# 10 + 107 + 118 + 24 = 259 does.
# THE DERIVATION IS UNAVOIDABLE, AND THAT IS NOW EVIDENCED FROM TWO SOURCES. First party:
# in the preserved raw scan the vulnerability object has NO severity key and its enclosing
# group carries max_severity "" - an empty string, not a number - so chain step 1 cannot
# resolve and step 2 has no label to read. Externally: the upstream advisory presents no
# severity row, no CVSS vector and no numeric score in either its human or its
# machine-readable form, and lists no alias and no CVE. Meanwhile AAP verification check 9
# closes the emitted vocabulary at critical|high|medium|low, so "unknown" is not emittable.
# impact_class is therefore a derivation of last resort, not a preference, and the record
# carries a 400+ character severity_basis stating exactly that.
#
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 23.6  ISSUE 6 (INFO) - one description used a prose version form
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# REPORTED: the same MAL-2025-22760 record wrote its version in prose - "The npm package
# http at 0.0.1-security ..." - while the other 258 descriptions use package@version.
# RESOLUTION: the description now reads "The npm package http@0.0.1-security is a
# confirmed-malicious package and a direct dependency of apps/api, so installing or loading
# it runs attacker-authored code in that process." - 178 characters, inside the 200 bound.
# VERIFIED: all 259 descriptions now carry the package@version form and zero prose-form
# version descriptions remain.
# TWO PROVENANCE POINTS THE QA RAISED ON THE SAME RECORD, both now declared next to the
# severity chain rather than left implicit. (1) cwe_all carries the inferred CWE-506
# alongside cwe, which is stated in cwe_basis rather than hidden; the record's four CWE
# provenance fields (cwe_resolved false, cwe_source and cwe_selection
# assigned_from_advisory_class, and a cwe_basis opening "INFERRED, not supplied by OSV")
# make the weaker provenance impossible to mistake for an OSV-supplied value. (2) The
# version 0.0.1-security comes from the SCANNER'S LOCKFILE MATCH, corroborated at
# yarn.lock lines 24667-24669, and NOT from the advisory, whose affected set is an
# unbounded SEMVER range with a single Introduced 0 event and no versions array at all.
# Additionally, because CWE-506 is a CLASS-level entry that its authority marks usable only
# with careful review, the most-specific-CWE obligation was re-tested against its four Base
# children (507 Trojan Horse, 510 Trapdoor, 511 Logic/Time Bomb, 512 Spyware); the advisory
# supplies no behavioural detail whatsoever, so none can be substantiated and the
# Class-level entry is the most specific classification the evidence supports.
#
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 23.7  ISSUE 7 (INFO) - seven category/CWE pairings depart from Block 8, four silently
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# REPORTED: 7 of the 40 Layer 3b records carry a CWE other than their category's canonical
# value - category 3 uses CWE-532 on three records, category 13 uses CWE-862 on one, and
# category 14 uses CWE-1049, CWE-639 and CWE-522 on three. Only the CWE-532 trio was
# justified in writing; the other four were tabulated without rationale.
# RESOLUTION, DOCUMENTATION ONLY - findings-layer-3b-taint.json is byte-identical, because
# in every case the emitted CWE is the CORRECT most-specific value and only its disclosure
# was missing. Two entries were added next to layer_3b_category_numbering: a
# layer_3b_category_cwe_pairing_rule that states the governing rule ONCE - category names
# the SINK COLUMN and is what check 7 keys on, while cwe names the MOST SPECIFIC WEAKNESS
# ACTUALLY PRESENT, and the two are deliberately allowed to disagree because the AAP's
# critical directives mandate most-specific-CWE classification while Block 8 fixes only one
# canonical CWE per category - and a layer_3b_category_cwe_deviations census that justifies
# all seven from the authoritative CWE definitions, which were re-read during this pass
# (catalogue version 4.20) rather than paraphrased from memory. 33 of the 40 records DO
# carry their category's canonical CWE.
# THE DECISIVE EXTERNAL FACT, worth stating because it is counter-intuitive: the CWE
# catalogue itself marks CWE-200 - the canonical value for category 14, from which three of
# the seven depart - as DISCOURAGED for mapping, on the stated grounds that confidentiality
# loss is a technical impact rather than a root-cause error, with the instruction "If an
# error or mistake causes information to be disclosed, then use the CWE ID for that error."
# All three category 14 deviations therefore move TOWARD the catalogue's own guidance.
# TWO CANDOUR POINTS carried into the census rather than smoothed over: CWE-522 is a
# SIBLING of CWE-200 (both are children of CWE-668), not a descendant, so it is narrower in
# subject matter and not lower in the same lineage; and CWE-1049 is a PARTIAL fit whose
# name and Reduce-Performance consequence match the finding while its description narrows
# to "a large number of joins and sub-queries" and its CISQ defaults of one million rows,
# five joins and three sub-queries are NOT met by a join-free over-fetch. CWE-1049 is still
# correct over CWE-200 because CWE-200 requires exposure "to an actor" and on that path the
# surplus rows never leave the process, and the finding is emitted at severity low with
# gateBlocking false, which is the honest weight for a fit of that strength.
# VERIFIED: the pairing comparison re-run over all 40 records finds 7 deviations and 0
# undocumented (was 4); all seven file:line citations in the new census were re-opened from
# disk and all seven resolve and are in range; check 7 (categories 1-19), check 8
# (gateBlocking on 40/40, non-blank demotionReason on 40/40) and check 16 (all 40 pairs
# anchored, re-proved against an independently rebuilt 6,564-pair anchor set) all pass.
#

# 24.1  THE KEY SET AS PUBLISHED

ledger_status_keys_published: 10 - the FOUR keys verification check 15 names verbatim
  (layer_0_status, layer_2_status, layer_3a_status, layer_4_status) PLUS the SIX
  short-form keys that are exactly the key set of findings-merged.json
  _summary.layer_status (layer_0, layer_1, layer_2, layer_3a, layer_3b, layer_4).
ledger_status_values_published: OK on all ten. Every value is drawn from the closed
  vocabulary {OK, ERROR}; no null, boolean or other token appears in any status slot.
ledger_why_the_agent_layers_are_now_RECORDED: check 15 asserts zero drift between this
  ledger and the merged summary, and that summary carries SIX layer_status keys while
  check 15 names only the four deterministic ones. A ledger holding four keys therefore
  forces the merge implementer to DERIVE the remaining two, and a derived status is
  exactly what IR3 exists to prevent - it cannot distinguish "the layer succeeded" from
  "the layer never ran". Recording layer_1 and layer_3b here removes that derivation
  step: the merge now populates all six keys from the ledger and invents nothing.
ledger_this_is_not_a_backfill: the prohibition is on backfilling an ABSENT key with a
  GUESS. Neither agent status is a guess. Each was computed at emit time from that
  layer's own artifacts - findings-layer-1-arch.json (126 records, categories 1..10, no
  category missing) with coverage-layer-1.txt (10 of 10 mandated lines persisted, 0
  malformed), and findings-layer-3b-taint.json (40 records, categories 1..19) with
  coverage-layer-3b.txt (19 of 19 persisted, 0 malformed). Neither artifact carries a
  coverage flag on any record. Had either artifact been absent or unparsable, the emitter
  would have WITHHELD the key rather than write one, preserving the check-15 signal.
ledger_status_keys_deliberately_NOT_added: layer_1_status and layer_3b_status. Check 15
  defines its *_status set over the PRE-AGENT steps, and widening that set would blur the
  boundary the check draws. The agent layers are recorded in the short form only.
ledger_harness_safety_invariant: every top-level key beginning with layer_ holds a status
  STRING. All non-status provenance lives under the single non-layer_-prefixed container
  "ledger", so a consumer that iterates layer_* keys and asserts the closed vocabulary
  cannot trip over an object-valued key. The previous revision violated this: it carried
  an object at top level under a layer_-prefixed name.

  unrelated defects appended to a rationale that is supposed to justify exactly one
  demotion. Every rewritten rationale now names the covering mitigation by file and line and
  states the single condition that caused the demotion, which is the requirement that it be

  rather than implied: role_in_this_run CURRENT snapshot, previous_baseline null,
  previous_baseline_present false, first_run true, warn_branch_reachable_this_run false, plus
  a consumer contract fixing the one-way order - read the PRIOR snapshot, decide the verdict,
  then persist the CURRENT snapshot - so a run can never compare itself against itself and a
  broken or partial run is never promoted to PRIOR. (3) run_id recomputed over the corrected

  rather than a layer output; and a FAIL-CLOSED consumer contract requiring a consumer to
  re-measure every attested output and to resolve any missing artifact, digest mismatch,
  absent status, out-of-vocabulary value or *_status-versus-short-form disagreement to ERROR,
  never to OK and never by defaulting. That closes the failure mode in which a stale or
  forged all-OK ledger is paired with stale layer artifacts to suppress the ERROR branch,
  which outranks BLOCK, WARN and PASS.
changed_findings_merged: five corrections, no re-merge. (1) 282 body fields mirrored from the
  corrected layer artifacts - 264 Layer 2 descriptions, 1 Layer 1 description and 17 Layer 3b
  demotionReason rationales - so merged body text and per-layer text remain identical
  everywhere, which is the property a reviewer diffs. (2) gate.warn_branch no longer compares
  this run against its own baseline: the published evaluation carried baseline_available true,
  a baseline total of 888 against a current total of 888 and a 0.0 percent delta, which is
  the overwrite-before-compare defect. It now records the true first-run state - PRIOR
  snapshot absent, reachable false, prior_total_findings null, current_total_findings 888 -
  and names the prohibition on self-comparison. The verdict is untouched: BLOCK, decided by
  the block branch on 16 gate-blocking Layer 3b findings, and WARN could not have fired in
  either framing. (3) The gate-blocking exploit for
  apps/api/v2/src/filters/http-exception.filter.ts:21 is now conditional: the rejected
  request's body and Authorization header are written to the process-wide log stream, and on
  to the remote transport WHENEVER the Axiom token and dataset are configured. The published
  text asserted remote egress unconditionally, which overstates a deployment-dependent
  condition; the finding, its severity and its gateBlocking value are unchanged. (4)
  provenance.generated_at_commit is replaced by a durable note: this report is written in the
  same commit that publishes it and therefore cannot carry that commit's own hash, so the
  measurement basis is audited_commit and the binding to the merged inputs is
  counts.by_layer_input_sha256. (5) Those four input digests were refreshed to the corrected
  arrays.
changed_internal_label_hygiene: 17 label residues removed from authored prose across three artifacts, and one

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 25.4  WHY NO LAYER RE-RAN, AND WHAT THAT PRESERVES
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# A text pass must not re-run a deterministic layer, because a re-run would replace the
# measured evidence a reviewer has already checked with a fresh measurement that happens to
# agree - or, worse, quietly disagree. The rule packs are pinned on disk and were not
# re-fetched; the scanners were not re-invoked; the inventories were not re-swept; the
# lockfile was not re-scanned. Consequently every exit code, wall-clock duration, files-
# scanned count and tool version recorded earlier in this file still describes the run that
# produced the published artifacts, and the audited revision remains
# e988138b24ffa49a0cd0f8dd7ac97caf2817fa1d.
read_only_guarantee_re_verified: 0 application source files, manifests, lockfiles, workflow
  definitions or container definitions were modified by this pass. The only tracked file
  outside security-audit/ this work has ever touched is .gitignore, and it is unchanged by
  this pass - additive by one comment and one entry, as recorded earlier.
